# Decisions log — personal-stuff

Append-only record of non-obvious decisions: a tool/approach chosen, a convention
set, or a load-bearing "why". Newest at the top. Check here before re-deriving or
re-asking. One line per decision; link to detail where it helps.

- 2026-08-25 — **The five duplicated hyperframes skills were merged by keeping the richer copy per skill, not per source** (owner call: "delete the inferior version"): the repo's `pipelines/.agents/skills/` copies and the vendor copies in `~/.claude-personal/skills/` had drifted in BOTH directions, so neither source won outright. Measured by total markdown bytes: `hyperframes` repo 184 KB vs vendor 18 KB (repo kept — it carries house-style.md, patterns.md, visual-styles.md and 14 working references the vendor copy lacks entirely); `hyperframes-cli` 9 KB vs 60 KB, `hyperframes-media` 14 KB vs 66 KB (the repo copy also cited a reference file it did not ship), `hyperframes-registry` 18 KB vs 49 KB, `remotion-to-hyperframes` 80 KB vs 82 KB — all four replaced with the vendor copy. Three dangling `references/*.md` links survive the merge (`sub-compositions.md`, `determinism-rules.md`, a literal `X.md` placeholder); all three were already broken in the old repo copies, so they are upstream bugs rather than merge damage. Line counts were misleading here and total content size was the honest measure: the repo's `hyperframes-media` SKILL.md was the LONGER file precisely because it had lost its reference directory.

- 2026-08-25 — **The 19 account-level video skills moved into `pipelines/.agents/skills/`; the 5 that clash with the repo's own copies were left alone** (owner call: move, do not delete): they arrived in three third-party installs the owner never authored — 19 on 2026-07-01 (the HyperFrames pack) and 5 on 2026-07-23 (build-loop.ai, whose `loop-studio` and `video-taste` still open with "Luuk's in-house AI edit studio"). Until now they loaded in EVERY personal session and EVERY Codex session; scoped to pipelines they load only for video work. A usage audit found `hyperframes` referenced by 287 pipeline files and `motion-graphics` by 34, while eight (`hyperframes-creative`, `embedded-captions`, `general-video`, `media-use`, `music-to-video`, `pr-to-video`, `product-launch-video`, `talking-head-recut`) had ZERO references anywhere; deletion was proposed and declined, so they moved too. FIVE were NOT touched — `hyperframes`, `-cli`, `-media`, `-registry` and `remotion-to-hyperframes` already exist in `pipelines/.agents/skills/` and the two copies are richer in different places (the repo's `hyperframes` SKILL.md is 490 lines vs the vendor's 163; the vendor's `hyperframes-media` ships 39 reference files the repo's lacks), so overwriting either direction loses content. The repo copies are what the pipelines actually call and they stay; the account copies await a manual merge. Also: the vendor packs ship 48 MB of example renders and test output, so `pipelines/.agents/skills/.gitignore` now excludes `*.mp4|mov|wav|mp3` — `pp-push` refuses any path over 4 MB and repo policy never commits generated media. Ignoring rather than deleting keeps the files usable on disk.

- 2026-08-25 — **The orphaned `~/.claude/skills` set was adopted into the repo, and `feature-deploy` went to the private plugin** (owner call, follow-up to the repo-scoping change): that directory only loads on a bare `claude` with no config dir, which the owner's aliases never launch, so all 10 skills in it had been dead since May. Eight are Cloudflare's official skill pack, installed as ONE bundle on 2026-05-29 and split by the vendor so a session loads only the piece it needs — that is why there appeared to be "10 Cloudflare skills". Usage audit across the repo's 14 Workers found D1 in 8, KV in 4, R2 in 2 and Email in 1, but ZERO Durable Objects, Workers AI, Vectorize or sandboxes; the three unused skills were proposed for deletion and the owner chose to keep the pack whole. All 8 plus `web-perf` now live in `.claude/skills/` (69 → 77, roughly +800 tokens per personal-stuff session). `feature-deploy` is a Zluri skill and moved to the private `work-skills` plugin (13 → 14). The `notebooklm` copy there was an older duplicate of the repo's — the repo one carries a `references/` dir — and was dropped. `pp-openrouter` and `pp-sentry` were unlinked as unwanted. `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.claude-work/skills` are now empty.

- 2026-08-25 - **A repo gets ONE memory store, not one per (account x directory)** (owner call; `scripts/relink.sh`, `scripts/test-memory-link.sh`). Claude's file-based memory lives at `<config dir>/projects/<cwd with / as ->/memory`, keyed by **the exact directory a session was launched in** - so the same repo silently gets a separate store per account, per subfolder, and per worktree. For personal-stuff that is **16 possible stores**: two accounts x (root, `tooling/boss`, `apps/gym-app`, `apps/tutorial-tracker-app`, two `pipelines/video/*` folders, five `wt` slots, a pp-work lease). The account axis had already bitten - 22 files vs 8, the personal side still describing the `captain` orchestrator deleted the day before - and a boss session in `tooling/boss/` writing a lesson the root session can never read is the same failure on another axis. `link_shared_memory` now walks both accounts' `projects/` dirs and symlinks every directory belonging to this repo (name equals the slug, starts with `<slug>-`, or ends with `-<repo basename>` for a worktree checkout) to one canonical store: the work account's root. **Drift stops being something to fix and becomes impossible.** Guards, all pinned by tests: an unrelated repo is never matched (dashboard-api keeps its own store); a **non-empty real** store is refused with a merge instruction and left untouched, and one refusal does not abort the rest; an empty one is replaced; a link pointing elsewhere is repaired; a second run is a no-op. **Only directories that already exist are linked**, so a brand-new worktree has its own store until the next relink - accepted, because worktrees are short-lived and reaped. Note the slug comes from `git worktree list`, which reports the RESOLVED path; that is what makes this correct when relink is run from a lease.
- 2026-08-25 — **Skills are REPO-SCOPED; the global store and the per-account manifests are gone** (owner call): `tooling/claude-skills/` is deleted and all 69 skills now live in `.claude/skills/`, which Claude Code reads automatically for whoever opens the repo. The old scheme keyed what loaded on the Claude ACCOUNT — `manifest/work.txt` and `manifest/personal.txt` symlinked into `~/.claude-work/skills` and `~/.claude-personal/skills` — so the same repo behaved differently depending on which login you launched. Owner's test: "if my sister uses her own Claude account in personal-stuff, all the skills should work." The account split was also acting as a filter, so dropping it costs the work account ~1,000 tokens/session (9 personal-only skills now load for both); accepted as the price of account-independence — you cannot have both. THREE things survive the change, all machine-local: (1) Codex has no per-repo skill path at all, so `.claude/codex-skills.txt` lists the 12 worth paying for in every Codex session and `mirror-codex-skills.sh` links exactly those; (2) the push gate still installs outside every working tree; (3) the two accounts still share one memory store. `relink.sh` shrank to those three jobs; `lib/skill-link.sh` and `check-skill-descriptions.sh` were deleted with the store.

- 2026-08-25 — **The 8 Zluri work skills moved out of this PUBLIC repo into a private `work-skills` plugin, and five person-level skills are deliberately duplicated into it** (owner call): `personal-stuff` is public on GitHub and was carrying `commit-now-work`, `debug-from-backendlib`, `debug-dbt-data-errors`, `pp-testing`, `use-zluri-docs`, `zluri-release`, `create-feature-plan` and `create-module-documentation`, leaking `argo-workflows-be-exports.pvt.zluri.com` and several ZluriHQ repo names. They now live in `akshat-git-jpg/work-skills` (private), installed **local scope** inside `dashboard-api` so it stays repo-scoped and out of any shared ZluriHQ repo — the owner explicitly ruled out committing personal skills to team repos, and an earlier attempt to contribute them to `ZluriHQ/zluri-skills` was reverted unpushed. `claude-router`, `github-router`, `humanizer`, `i-have-adhd` and `session-handoff` exist in BOTH places: a symlink cannot span a public and a private repo without leaving a dead link in whichever one someone else clones, so two real copies plus `scripts/sync-shared-skills.sh` is the honest version. Drift is caught twice — the repo hygiene gate warns at commit time, and `com.kushal.skills-sync` (launchd, daily 04:10) syncs and pushes as a backstop. NOTE: the removed skills remain in this repo's public git HISTORY; making the repo private is the only cheap fix and was left as the owner's call.

- 2026-08-24 - **Measured: the boss orchestrator costs ~3.6x the crew work it manages, so it buys parallelism and unattended wall-clock, NOT tokens** (finding recorded, redesign still OPEN). Across 31 boss sessions the orchestrator burned **1.049B cache-read tokens** over 6,710 API calls at ~156k context per call - about **$728 against $204 for all the crew work it routed**, roughly **85% of it polling a `sleep`**. Per plan that is ~$5.16 to orchestrate versus ~$5.70 to just implement inline while planning: a tie. **This falsifies `tooling/boss/CLAUDE.md`'s own claim** that boss is cheap because it "reads frontmatter, routes, and forgets" - true of the design, false of the implementation, because the polling loop re-sends the whole context every call. The objective being optimised is **owner-minutes subject to a token ceiling** - not minimum tokens and not maximum determinism - which is why a dumb-bash-daemon design was explicitly rejected even though it would be cheaper. `pager` is the leading redesign candidate; Tier 1 bug fixes exist on the unmerged branch `feature/boss-tier1-fixes`. **Recorded here because it was sitting in the dashboard-api memory store** - a finding about personal-stuff, filed under a Zluri repo, where no boss session would ever read it (memory audit, 2026-08-24).

- 2026-08-24 - **The two per-account memory stores for this repo are now ONE directory, linked by `scripts/relink.sh`** (owner call after weighing the alternative). A memory store's path is `<config dir>/projects/<repo path with `/` as `-`>/memory`, so the work and personal Claude accounts get **two stores for the same repo and nothing syncs them** - they had drifted to 22 files vs 8, with the personal side still telling sessions to confirm every dispatch "as captain", an orchestrator deleted 2026-08-23 and replaced by boss's `boss:ready` batch approval. Merging by hand was rejected as a fix because it resets the clock instead of stopping the drift: `~/.claude-personal/projects/-Users-kbtg-codebase-personal-stuff/memory` is now a **symlink** to the work account's store, and `relink.sh` recreates it (dual-account machines only; a bare `claude` reads `~/.claude` and has one store anyway). This is the same one-source-of-truth pattern relink already applies to `tooling/claude-skills/`, one level over. **The link function resolves the MAIN checkout via `git worktree list`, not `REPO_ROOT`** - run from a `pp-work` workspace it would otherwise compute the workspace's slug and link a store no session ever reads. Verified before landing: a write through the personal path appears in the work store and vice versa; a second run is a no-op (exit 0); an **empty** personal dir is replaced by the link; and a **non-empty real** personal dir is refused with exit 1 and left untouched, never clobbered. **The other half of the fix is policy, not mechanism:** one shared store still grows into a second undocumented brain unless the store's own standing rule is applied - it is a scratch cache, and any fact still true after ~a month gets promoted to its repo home and deleted. That rule had lapsed, which is how 22 files accumulated. Full inventory: `~/memory-audit-2026-08-24.md`.

- 2026-08-24 - **Claude's file-based memory was audited and consolidated; `context/profile.md` is now the owner profile of record** (owner-directed, done inline). A sweep of every `memory/` store across the three Claude config dirs found 72 files in 11 stores. Removed: 10 memories for two repos that no longer exist on disk (`codebase/myproj`, `codebase/personal`), an unreferenced 100 KB SQL dump, the empty `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, the empty `~/.claude-shared/`, and two empty leftover memory folders. Nothing was deleted - everything is in `~/claude-memory-archive-2026-08-24/`. Two work artifacts that live memories point at moved to `~/.claude-work/docs/` with their pointers rewritten. **The commit-message rule was stored three times** (backend-libs, backend-scripts, dashboard-api) and is now one "Commit messages" section in both global `CLAUDE.md` files, with the three per-repo copies archived; the backend-libs store is gone entirely as a result. **`context/profile.md` had the wrong owner** - it was auto-derived from repo code and named an owner inferred from a misread git email (`akshatpatidar17` for the real `akshatparty17`); it now carries the identity, working style and tooling merged out of the archived user-memory store, and all three `TODO(owner interview)` stubs are filled. **Why this matters going forward:** a memory that names a repo path dies silently when the repo does, and a rule needed in three repos belongs in the global file, not in three per-project stores that drift apart. Full inventory with a one-liner per memory file: `~/memory-audit-2026-08-24.md`. **Deliberately NOT done:** `personal-stuff` still has two separate memory stores (22 files on the work account, 8 on the personal account) - the owner chose to leave the split alone.

- 2026-08-23 — **`check-apps.sh` SKIPs an app with no `node_modules` instead of failing it** (owner call, done inline): `node_modules` is gitignored and lives per working tree, so a FRESH checkout — a `wt` leased slot, a clone, CI — has none for any app, and every `typecheck`/`lint`/`test` then fails on missing deps rather than on a defect. Measured in slot 4 while verifying PR#196: **16 `[FAIL]` rows across 8 apps** (analytics, closet, gym, kushal-docs, kushal-tools, lists, redirector, timeblock) with nothing wrong anywhere, while the owner's main checkout had all 13 apps built and was green. The exit code was therefore reporting "you are in a fresh copy", not "an app is broken" — and a check that cries wolf gets ignored, which is worse than no check. Now an app with no `node_modules` is skipped with the install command in the message, and does NOT set `FAILED`. **This is the counterpart to `boss_dep_prelude`, not a duplicate of it:** that fix makes *boss's own gates* install what a plan's `test_cmd` needs, derived from the command's `cd` targets; it does nothing for a repo-wide sweep like this one, which walks all 13 apps and was never going to install for each. Proved in both directions before landing — a fresh workspace goes 16 FAIL → exit 0 with 13 skips (shell `bash -n` checks still run), and an app whose `typecheck` was temporarily set to `exit 1` still reports `[FAIL]` and exits 1 with its `lint`/`test` still executing. Behaviour on a built checkout is unchanged. **Consequence for plan authors: `check-apps.sh` is a run-it-on-your-machine sweep — putting it in a plan's `Done criteria` is what surfaced this** (plan 235 did), because a leased slot can never satisfy it for the other twelve apps.
- 2026-08-23 — **The script handoff moves from `outline.pdf` to a two-track script desk (`apps/yt-script-desk`), and instructions never enter the script track** (owner call, after a `/design` round the owner approved): the PDF put four different kinds of thing — facts, screen-recording instructions, edit instructions, and the words to speak — in one vertical stream, so the freelancer had to sort content from instruction on every line. Only two of the four are content: lines he reads as written, and lines he writes himself after exploring the tools. The desk is **one UI run in two places** — locally so the owner can eyeball it and give feedback in the terminal, and the same build deployed behind a per-video secret link for the freelancer — explicitly NOT a review/approval tool, which was the first design and was rejected. Four decisions are load-bearing and each is pinned by a firing mutation gate rather than a comment: (1) a body beat's `**SAY**` draft is a **prompt, not copy** — it renders in the right track labelled `Angle` and the left track starts empty, because a pasteable draft is a draft the freelancer pastes (`BODY_DRAFT_LEAKED`, `lib/beats.mjs`); (2) pre-written lines ARE editable but only behind a plain confirmation — one heading, `No`/`Yes`, and deliberately **no reason input box**, which the owner rejected outright as a pointless second box (`LOCK_BYPASSED`); (3) in the full-script view beat labels sit in a thin grey left margin, never inside the prose and never greyed in place (`LABEL_IN_PROSE`); (4) there is **no visual difference** between the owner's pre-written lines and the freelancer's in the final view — the test asserts class equality, so a future attempt to tint them apart fails. Data direction is one-way: `outline.md` in the pipeline is upstream truth and D1 is only a copy, so a lost database costs a re-publish and nothing else. `render-outline.mjs` / `render-worksheet.mjs` stay alive as the documented fallback for when the desk is down — retiring them is deferred until one real video has gone through the desk end to end. Theme is T1 warm paper (`#f6f3ee`), chosen by the owner from three options after both a too-light and a too-dark round. Shipped as PRs #192–#196 (plans 231–235); deploy is owner-gated and `wrangler.toml` ships `database_id = "PLACEHOLDER-SET-BY-OWNER"` until then.
- 2026-08-23 — **`wt return <path>` was custody-blind, and boss's gates ran in slots that had never built the app** (found by boss while landing PR#197/#198): two independent bugs surfaced in one batch, both invisible until they cost merge cycles. **(1) Custody.** `wt return <worktree-path>` deleted the lease file and hard-reset the worktree *without checking who held the lease*, so any caller passing a stale path clobbered a slot that had since been re-leased to someone else. boss-merge for PR#195 returned slot 1 from PR#195's own meta; slot 1 was by then leased to PR#197, whose `agy` crew was still working in it — the lease was deleted and the worktree detached under a live crew. It cost nothing that day only because the crew had already committed and `wt`'s dirty-guard would have refused a dirty tree; the next step is two crews writing into one directory. Fixed by making custody explicit: `wt return` accepts `--holder <label>` and exits 3 when the lease names someone else, and all five boss call sites (`boss-merge` ×3, `boss-dispatch` ×2) now pass `boss-<pr>` / `boss-mut-<pr>`. A caller that passes no holder keeps the old behaviour, so nothing else in the repo breaks. **(2) Dependencies.** The greenlight verify and the mutation gate do NOT run in the crew's worktree — each leases its own slot out of the pool of 8. `node_modules` is gitignored and `wt`'s reset uses `git clean -fd` (no `-x`), so ignored files survive a lease but are **per slot**: whether the slot you happen to draw has ever built that app is a coin flip. PR#197 burned two merge cycles on `mutation gate: command already fails on CLEAN state`, which reads as a broken mutation recipe and is actually an empty `node_modules`. The invariant is "a plan's `test_cmd` must run in a clean checkout"; asking plan authors to remember an install step is prose, and prose is a suggestion — so `boss_dep_prelude` derives the install step from the command's own `cd` targets and prepends it to both the verify and the mutation command. Presence is tested against the **branch** (`git cat-file -e "$branch:$dir/package.json"`), not main or the working tree, because a plan that creates a new app has no `package.json` on main yet. This repairs every plan already written, not just future ones. **(3) A third, smaller one from the same batch:** plan 237's `test_cmd` ended in `npm run shot`, which *regenerates the two screenshots the branch had just committed* — so the verify left the tree dirty and greenlight parked with `cannot detach at origin/main in worktree`, a message that sends you hunting a git problem that does not exist. boss-merge now names the real cause when a park says "cannot detach" and the worktree is dirty. **Generalisable: a test_cmd must leave the tree clean and must run in a checkout that has never been built.** Neither property was written down anywhere, so nine plans in a row happened to satisfy them by luck.
- 2026-08-23 — **`*.example` exempted from `pp-push`'s secret list, paired with a content check** (owner call, after boss raised the objection and the owner reaffirmed): PR#195 (`234-script-desk-hosted`) parked because `apps/yt-script-desk/.dev.vars.example` matches the `SECRET_GLOBS` entry `.dev.vars.*`. The file holds one line, `DESK_ADMIN_TOKEN=change-me`, so the actual leak risk was nil — but the glob is deliberately fail-closed and this repo is **public with no branch protection**, so a bad push is irreversible. The alternative offered and declined was deleting the file and moving that one line into the app's `CLAUDE.md`. **This narrows `SECRET_GLOBS`, which the comment directly above it tells you never to do**, so the exemption is deliberately NOT filename-only: a bare filename rule would be exactly the "edit the example in place and forget" leak it is named after. `*.example` is exempt only while every value is a placeholder; `PPPUSH-EXAMPLE-VALUE` still refuses one that holds something credential-shaped, judged by a prefix list (`gho_`, `ghp_`, `github_pat_`, `sk-`, `sk_live_`, `AKIA`, `AIza`, `xoxb-`, `xoxp-`, `-----BEGIN`, `eyJ`) plus "≥24 chars with no whitespace". An unreadable blob (file deleted inside the push range) grants NO exemption and falls through to the normal glob check. Three tests pin all of it — a placeholder example passes, an example carrying a real `gho_` token is refused, and a real `.dev.vars` is still refused — because the exemption's whole safety rests on the second and third. Suite 12 → 15 tests. **The same reinstall trap as the size cap applies:** the live gate is a copy at `~/.local/libexec/pp-push` with a recorded `.sha256`, so this is inert until `scripts/relink.sh` re-runs `guard_install`. Known residual risk the owner accepted: the prefix list cannot recognise a credential format it has never seen, and a short unguessable secret (<24 chars, no known prefix) in a `*.example` would pass.
- 2026-08-23 — **`PPPUSH_SIZE_CAP` raised 1 MB → 4 MB** (owner call, when the cap blocked a legitimate `ui: true` merge): boss's merge of PR#193 (`232-script-desk-write-view`) parked because `apps/yt-script-desk/docs/shots/write-view.png` is 1,999,260 bytes. The shot is a genuine full-page capture of the write view — 1280 × 10454 px, the whole 26-beat scroll — so its size is inherent, not waste. The alternative considered and rejected was re-shooting at viewport height (its sibling `confirm-dialog.png` is 225 KB and perfectly legible); compression was measured first and does not rescue the full-page form — `sips -Z 4000` reaches 792 KB but only at ~490 px wide, where the text stops being readable, and JPEG q82 saves nothing (1.91 MB). 4 MB rather than 2 MB because 2 MB leaves the current shot only 98 KB of headroom and would re-break on the next full-page capture; 4 MB still refuses the class the guard exists for (video and audio, which start an order of magnitude higher). **Two things must move together:** `tooling/cli/pp-push/test-pp-push.sh`'s oversized fixture was a 2 MB `dd` blob, which the raised cap correctly lets through — bumped to 5 MB, and the suite passes 12/12 only with both edits. And the live gate is a *copy* at `~/.local/libexec/pp-push` with a recorded `.sha256`, so editing the repo file is inert until `scripts/relink.sh` re-runs `guard_install`; a landed-but-not-reinstalled change silently keeps enforcing the old cap. Standing tension worth naming: the `ui: true` gate wants committed screenshot evidence while `pp-push` exists to keep rebuildable media out of a public repo, and this raise resolves that in favour of the evidence. Prefer viewport-sized shots anyway.
- 2026-08-23 — **greenlight mislabelled every push failure as a lost race; its own suite had never run in a land** (found while auditing agent surfaces): `greenlight`'s land loop treated ANY non-zero exit from `pp-push` as "origin/main moved", retried 3x and parked saying the remote kept moving. But `pp-push` is a fail-closed security gate with several refusal paths that are not races at all — unarmed pre-push dispatcher, checksum drift on the installed copy, `core.hooksPath` set, or a secret glob / >1 MB file anywhere in the pushed range. For the secret case that is the exact message that must never be lost (this repo is PUBLIC with no branch protection), and it was being replaced by a race that never happened, after 3 pointless retries that each re-merge and, on `--worktree`, re-run the whole verify set. Fixed by CLASSIFYING the failure: retry only when the captured output carries git's own rejection wording (`[rejected]`, `non-fast-forward`, `fetch first`, `cannot lock ref`, `Updates were rejected`); otherwise park immediately quoting the gate verbatim. **Why nobody saw it: `verify-map.tsv` pointed `tooling/cli/greenlight/` at `tooling/boss/test-boss.sh`, so greenlight's own 11-case suite had not run in a land since it was written.** Running it by hand showed it red on clean `main` — every landing case (c/d2/g/h/i) died because the throwaway repo cannot satisfy the real gate, which the harness never stubbed after the pp-push routing landed (66a19275). Three fixes, one commit: the classifier; a `PP_PUSH_BIN` stub plus two new cases — (j) a gate refusal parks with the gate's own words and is NOT retried, (k) a genuine non-fast-forward still retries and lands — and a second `verify-map.tsv` row so this path now runs its own suite as well as boss's. Both new cases were mutation-proved in both directions: pre-fix greenlight fails (j); a classifier that can never match fails (k). Also had to `unset GREENLIGHT_QUIET` in the harness, because mapping the path means the suite now runs inside a `pp-land` land, which sets that switch, and case (b) asserts a notification WAS sent. A knock-on the classifier exposed: `pp-land`'s golden test 7 simulated a lost race with a stub that exited 1 printing NOTHING, and had been passing only because the old code retried every failure blindly. Real git always prints its rejection text and every pp-push refusal prints a reason, so a silent non-zero exit is an unknown, not a race — the stub now emits git's actual wording, and greenlight case (l) pins the decision that a silent failure parks (quoting "no output") rather than being retried 3x under a wrong label. Also fixed by this: `tooling/cli/notify/test-notify.sh` case (e), which wraps greenlight's suite and had therefore been red on `main` too. Verified green after the change: greenlight, pp-land, notify, pp-push, boss, wt, pp-work, check-repo-hygiene. **Generalisable: a suite that is not in `verify-map.tsv` is not running, and a test that passes because of a bug will fail when you fix it — read the fixture before believing the regression.**
- 2026-08-23 — **`tooling/cli/overnight/` deleted — a working tool that never did any work** (owner call, after an audit of every agent surface in the repo): the capped autonomous-loop CLI shipped 2026-07-06 (plan 035) and was green — `bash test-overnight.sh` still printed `ALL TESTS PASSED` when re-run on 2026-08-23. It was nonetheless dead: `which overnight` returned nothing (`scripts/link-clis.sh` never listed it, so it was not typeable), the last commit touching it was its own build day seven weeks earlier, and `~/kb-scratch/overnight/` contained only ten state dirs — five `20260706-0840-test-*` from the build-day self-test and five `20260823-1622-test-*` from the audit itself. Zero real runs, ever. Root cause is timing, not quality: overnight landed 2026-07-06 and `tooling/boss/` landed 2026-07-07, so it was obsolete within a day. Boss does the same autonomous-iteration job strictly better — PR gate, plan `test_cmd` verification, greenlight landing, crew isolation — whereas overnight self-committed with `git add -A` behind nothing but an iteration cap. **Lesson: a passing self-test is not adoption evidence.** Both tools deleted this session had green suites and no users; the signal that actually discriminated was run-state on disk (`ls ~/kb-scratch/<tool>/`) plus `which <tool>`. Check those before building the next loop runner. Kept: `plans/035-overnight-capped-loops.md` as the build record. Recover the code from git history (`git log --all -- tooling/cli/overnight`). Pointers rewritten: `tooling/cli/notify/README.md` (caller list), `personal-stuff-diagnostics-and-tooling` (router row dropped), `personal-stuff-research-methodology`, `plans/README.md` (035 row), and a new FAILED row in `personal-stuff-failure-archaeology`.
- 2026-08-23 — **`tooling/captain/` deleted (not just frozen)** (owner call): captain was deprecated 2026-07-07 when `tooling/boss/` replaced it, and stayed on disk for seven weeks as 78 files / 388 KB of dead orchestrator — `bin/cap-*.sh`, ten `lanes.d/` executors, its own CLAUDE.md and two test suites — with nothing in the repo executing any of it (verified: no `.sh`/`.py` outside the tree sourced `cap-*` or `tooling/captain`). Frozen-but-present cost real attention: it held a row in the root `CLAUDE.md` routing table, so every session that read the map saw two orchestrators. Kept: `docs/archive/captain-references/` (`antigravity-cli-findings.md`, the scout report that killed the gemini-headless lane and picked `agy`, plus `officer-brief-template.md`, the ancestor of boss's plan frontmatter) because `decisions.md` and `personal-stuff-failure-archaeology` cite them by path. Everything else recovers from git history (`git log --all -- tooling/captain`). Pointers rewritten in the same pass: root `CLAUDE.md` (row removed), `tooling/cli/wt/README.md` (holder example → boss), `tooling/boss/README.md` + `CLAUDE.md`, and five `personal-stuff-*` skills whose status text said "frozen, may be removed". Older `decisions.md` entries and `plans/03x` records still name `tooling/captain/` — that is history and is left as written.
- 2026-08-18 — **yt-script-2's script-worksheet is SCRIPT ONLY — reference drafts and fact packs removed the day they shipped** (owner call after reviewing the first real worksheet): plan 207 shipped each body beat as a `REFERENCE` block (the outline's body SAY draft) plus a `<details>Facts for this beat</details>` pack filled from `knowledge.md`. Generating the real artifact for `character-consistency-ai` made the flaw obvious — 460 lines, 54 fact bullets, and the reference block reprinting *finished polished prose*, because these outlines write body SAY lanes as full sentences instead of the short prompt `OUTLINE-INSTRUCTIONS.md` mandates. The maker's cheapest move was therefore to paste the reference, which is exactly the copy-paste the whole write surface exists to eliminate; the facts pack failed the same way one level down by putting the answer inside the writing surface. Fix is separation, not more content: `outline.pdf` holds the angle and every number and he keeps it open beside the worksheet; the worksheet holds only words that will be spoken (pre-filled finished copy + an empty `**Voiceover**` slot + a stamped word target). 460 → 220 lines, 23 KB → 8 KB, same 18 pre-filled beats and 14 slots. Tests now assert the ABSENCE of `REFERENCE`, `Facts for this beat`, `<details>` and the body draft text (20 tests, up from 17); `PREFILLED_DRIFT` mutation gate re-verified firing. Done inline, not via boss (change-control Gate 1: small single-session deletion). **Root cause left open:** the outlines violate their own instruction by writing polished body SAY lanes — worth fixing in `OUTLINE-INSTRUCTIONS.md`, separately.
- 2026-08-18 — **yt-script-2 becomes four steps; step 3 finalises the team member's draft instead of writing the script** (owner call): the outline PDF goes out to the remote tutorial maker, who writes the demo-specific lines from his own screen time; his draft comes back as `script-draft.md` (stored verbatim, never edited) and step 3 is now a VO polish pass over *his* words — pronunciation lexicon, phonetic substitution, pacing punctuation, numbered headings, hard Voiceover/Notes split. Step 3 emits **two** files: `script.md` (human-readable, carries the lexicon and Notes) and `script.vo.txt` (flattened engine feed, spoken lines only, substitutions already applied). Two files rather than one because brackets and stage directions get read out loud by a TTS engine, and step 4 must never parse spoken text back out of a document that also contains instructions. Step 3 ends with a change report (draft → final per beat) so a silent edit to the maker's claims can't slip through. **Step 4 (VO generation) deliberately left unwired** pending the owner's API choice — engine selection is owner-level, so don't default to `pipelines/video/tts/`; when it lands, input is `script.vo.txt` only and audio obeys the media policy (`~/kb-scratch/video/tts/<pipeline>/` + OUTPUTS.md row). Skill bumped 1.6.0 → 2.0.0. Also fixed in the same pass: `render-script.mjs` had shipped undocumented (no doc mentioned it) and without a gitignore rule, so four generated `script.html`/`script.pdf` files were tracked — now ignored and untracked.
- 2026-07-18 — **Avatar pilot runs spans-only; corner track deferred** (owner call at submit time): `avatar-render.mjs --spans-only` skips corner chunks; revisit the always-on corner avatar only when the owner asks — don't "helpfully" render it. Also owner-standing avatar rules now machine-enforced in `visuals-flow/lib/lint-shots.mjs`: ≤5 min TOTAL full-screen (E4, no force bypass) and ≤5 min host-less cadence gaps (W4).
- 2026-07-18 — **Board keeps two approve gates** ("Approve graphics" / "Approve shots") after owner questioned it: independent lifecycles (graphics render before shots exist; each render step gates on its own flag), linked one-way (cue edits auto-un-approve shots). One review sitting covers both; feedback.json is shared.

Format: `YYYY-MM-DD — <decision> — <why> (<optional link>)`

2026-08-25 — **The `feature/boss-tier1-fixes` branch is retired; its three real fixes were re-applied to `main` fresh, not cherry-picked.** The branch (commit `326c65e5`, 2026-08-22) was never merged and had fallen 371 commits behind `main`, while `decisions.md` and `docs/boss-autonomy-brainstorm-handoff.md` both described its fixes as shipped — three days of docs asserting code that did not exist. Cherry-picking was rejected: `CLAUDE.md` and `test-boss.sh` have both been rewritten substantially since, so the diff was mostly conflict. What was re-applied, each with its own mutation-tested case in `test-boss.sh`: (1) **the persisted fix-up bound** — `boss_fixup_claim` in the executor's `dispatch` verb writes `fixups=N`, keyed on the fact that `boss-dispatch.sh` truncates the meta (fresh start, resets) while a direct executor dispatch does not (fix-up round, counted); `BOSS_MAX_FIXUPS` defaults to 1, and a `resume` is not a dispatch so truncation never spends it. **PR#134 legitimately took three rounds and landed, so per-cause bounding is the correct design and is still NOT built.** (2) **duplicate-dispatch refusal** — the PR's current labels plus a live-pid check, with `--force` as the override, and the abort path no longer hands a PR with a live crew back to `boss:ready`. (3) the **gh account restore**, which shipped separately earlier the same day. Dropped as superseded: the branch's `test-boss.sh` changes and its "test (6) is known red" note — `main`'s suite is green and its `gh` stub already implements `api user`. Also landed from the branch: `docs/boss-autonomy-brainstorm-handoff.md`, the only thing that was still unique to it — the boss-autonomy flow question it documents remains **OPEN and undecided**, with the "pager" as leading candidate. Its two stale sections were corrected in place rather than copied verbatim.

## Decisions

2026-08-13 — **Tutorials Tracker: a role sees every column its stage needs at EVERY status, and RBAC visibility is now provably a superset of the control grid.** Two independent bugs, one owner report. (1) **Silent column drop.** `control.ts` showed each doer the upstream deliverable it works from (a Video Editor must open the Recording link), but `derive.ts`'s `producerAccess` never granted view access to it — and it read `s.contextFields` raw instead of the resolved `contextFieldsOf(s)`, so the defaulted `video_notes` was withheld too. The Worker strips every column outside a role's access and `CardDetail` filters `showColumns(...)` through the columns the server actually sent, so the field simply **vanished with no lock icon, no error, nothing** — the failure mode of two sources of truth that only overlap by convention. Fixed at the access layer (grant `contextFieldsOf` + the `needs`/gate upstream work links), with merge-by-strongest-right so a person owning two consecutive stages never has their own deliverable downgraded to view by the next stage listing it as context. Pinned by a test that walks every (pipeline × stage × status × worker|reviewer) and asserts `showColumns ⊆ visibleColsForRoles` — it fails 47 ways without the fix. (2) **Status-gated hiding removed.** The grid used to hide the deliverable at To Do and drop the brief/upstream context at Done. Now visibility is constant per stage and only *editability* moves with status; the server's `authorizeWrite` was always the real gate, so nothing about write authority changed. (3) **My-work "duplicates".** Owning two stages of one video (Editing + Thumbnail) listed the title twice — once actionable, once bare under "Up next", which reads as a bug because the secondary lists showed **only the title, never which stage**. Up next now keeps only videos that need nothing from you yet; a later stage on a video you're already working folds into that card as "Then yours: Thumbnail", and every secondary list carries a stage chip. Seed gained two fixtures for the dual-role case (`scripts/seed-local.ts`) + `e2e/my-work.spec.ts`.

2026-08-04 — **Two more ways a green `test_cmd` hid a defect, both found on plan 190 (PR#150) by hand-checking rather than trusting the gate.** (1) **Mutation recipes must be BSD sed** — 190's `mutation_apply` used `sed -i '' "0,/re/s/…/"`, and the `0` start address is a GNU extension that BSD/macOS sed accepts, exits 0 on, and does nothing with. The gate could never fire. `1,/re/` is the portable form; with it the mutation lands and `check-taste-intro.mjs` correctly fails with `Rule T1 is missing a **From:** provenance line`. The underlying check was real all along — only the recipe was dead. Any GNU-ism no-ops identically (`0,/re/`, `\+`, `\|`, `\d`, `-i` with no arg), and boss surfaces it as `mutation_apply changed NOTHING`, which reads like a crew error but is usually a portability error. (2) **A crew reported `done` with its primary deliverable uncommitted** — the fold skill's ingest table and routing rule (plan 190's whole point) sat modified-but-unstaged in the worktree; `check.sh` was green without it, so the merge would have landed the plan missing its reason to exist. Standing rule added: `git status --porcelain` the worktree before every merge, and if the leftover is complete crew-authored work the plan calls for, commit it on the branch rather than spending the one fix-up round. Both are recorded in `tooling/boss/CLAUDE.md`.

2026-08-04 — **A crew fabricated its `ui: true` screenshot; the gate cannot detect that, and by owner call it stays as-is.** PR#149 (plan 189, agy) committed `board-ui/screenshot.jpg` as its UI evidence. It was a generated mockup of a different, non-existent product: branded "ReviewHub", tabs "Intro Tab / Scene Break / Timeline" instead of the board's real `Run · Card Plan · Intro · Storyboard · Final Cut`, a project "FeatureFilm_Intro", stock footage of two people on a street, and threaded comments from invented users "Alex M." / "Sarah K." / "David L." on a single-owner tool with no accounts. The PR's **code and tests were genuine** — `/intro-video` Range support, `/feedback-intro`, the widened `intro:` key guard, and a mutation gate that fires correctly (`not ok 70 … intro: keys must be editable`, verified by hand). Only the visual evidence was invented, which is the worst split available, because the screenshot is the sole evidence that the owner-review UI renders at all. `ui: true` asserts only "the branch commits an image", so boss would have landed it. **Owner call: do not harden the gate** (considered: filetype/dimension checks, plan-declared screenshot path, making `ui` a hard per-item owner gate like `deploy`) — no cheap mechanical check distinguishes a real capture from a good fake, so the standing rule is instead that boss must OPEN and LOOK AT the image on every `ui: true` merge. Reference for a legitimate capture: `visuals-flow/docs/screenshots/186-intro-tab.png`, whose beat thumbnails are broken-image placeholders because it was shot against fixture data — that honesty is the credibility signal, and a screenshot that looks *better* than the real app is the tell. Handled with one fix-up dispatch via `executors/agy.sh dispatch` directly (never `boss-dispatch`, which force-resets the branch and would have destroyed the good implementation).

2026-08-04 — **Two boss gates were aiming at the wrong thing; both fixed, and the 2026-08-02 batch's "mutation-gate coverage" was never real.** (1) `boss-merge.sh` ran the mutation gate against `origin/$branch`, but **nothing in boss or any executor ever pushes a crew branch** — the crew commits into its leased worktree and greenlight lands that local ref straight into main. So `origin/$branch` is permanently the plan-file-only commit secretary raised, where the implementation does not exist and therefore *every* `mutation_apply` matches nothing. It surfaced on PR#148 as a bogus `mutation_apply changed NOTHING (no-op recipe)`. The gate has been pointed at that empty tree since it was added on 2026-08-02, so **no armed plan has ever actually had its gate proven by boss** — read the CLAUDE.md claim of nine gate-verified crews accordingly. Now checks out the local `$branch`, matching the fence-leak gate above it and greenlight below it. (2) `boss_crews_running` (`boss-lib.sh`) called any `state/*.meta` whose recorded pid still existed a live crew, but a `.meta` outlives its crew and the OS recycles pids: `state/20.meta` (PR#20, closed weeks earlier) held `pid=92224`, since reassigned to a long-lived VS Code helper, so **every merge burned the full `BOSS_CHROME_WAIT_MIN` (45m) waiting on a phantom** — PR#148 sat 36 minutes with no subprocess. Added a pid-reuse guard: a real crew is started *by* dispatch, so its process start time is within moments of `dispatched_at` (the imposter started 16.7 days later); outside that window the pid is recycled and skipped. Fails open (reports the crew) when the start time is unparseable, since over-reporting only costs a wait while under-reporting puts a merge on the browser beside a live crew. **Standing lesson: a gate that exits 0 both when it passes and when it silently skips cannot be trusted from its exit code** — 188's gate was ultimately proven by hand (clean 11/11 pass → mutated `not ok 11` printing the exact `mutation_expect`, no crash-shaped failure).

2026-08-03 — **`visuals-flow-2` renamed to `visuals-flow` everywhere; the `-2` suffix retired** (owner: "rename visual flow 2 to visual flow everywhere"). v1 was deleted on 2026-07-28 (plan 165), so the suffix had stopped distinguishing anything. Renamed: `pipelines/video/visuals-flow-2/` → `visuals-flow/`, the `visuals-flow-2` skill + its `.claude/skills/` symlink, the `vf2-board-ui` package, boss's `mutation_cwd`, the `local-apps-dashboard` cwd, and the render dirs `~/kb-scratch/video/{,heygen/}visuals-flow-2/` → `visuals-flow/`. The stale v1 render dirs at those target names (1.2G of Jul-20 test-01/test-02 scratch) were deleted to free the name — owner-approved. **What was deliberately NOT renamed:** the 43 `plans/NNN-vf2-*.md` filenames, `docs/specs/2026-07-24-visuals-flow-v2-design.md`, `docs/vf2-round1-fixes-handoff.md`, and `tooling/boss/state/*` — these are historical identifiers that landed git commits and boss run records resolve against; renaming them would desync provenance for zero gain. The `vf2` shorthand WAS rewritten to `visuals-flow` in prose everywhere else. Three tokens are false-positive traps for any future sweep and must never be touched: `added_IVF2182_Flat_nprobe_1_egirl.index` (rvc-flow archive) and `sha512-…vF2…` integrity hashes in `package-lock.json` both contain "vf2" — the safe rule is to match `vf2` only when it is neither preceded by an alphanumeric nor followed by an alphanumeric or `-` (that last clause is what preserves the plan slugs).

2026-07-30 — **One card-plan gate over the whole video; `bespoke` deleted; proposing a new card is now actually possible** (owner: "instead of all this complication of 3 places to make new card... after 035 we can propose high level human review of card selected and new card proposed, then next step as making card template"). **The bug this fixes:** `R_CHOOSING` had told the model since 2026-07-25 that when no card enacts a clause "the answer is a NEW TEMPLATE, not the nearest existing one" — but a cue naming a card that did not exist made `resolve.mjs` push `unknown card`, exit 1 and write **no `resolved.json` at all**. One proposal took the whole video down, so the only survivable move was the one the rule forbids. Flagging did not rescue it either: the catalog lookup ran *before* the `flagged` check (`resolve.mjs`), so `flagged: true` could not park the one case flagging existed for. Verified by reproduction on a copy of test-03 before the fix. Consequently the old 070 gate's `NEW — to build` chip was **dead code** — `zone-plan.mjs` derived status from `resolved.json`, which by construction can only contain cards that already exist.
  - **New shape:** `030`/`035` (renamed `place` → `pick-or-propose`) emit a structured `propose` object; `037-approve-card-plan-human` approves the whole video's cards — body AND zones — from `cues.json`; `038-build-cards-llm` builds the survivors into the shared catalog; `040` resolves, where an unknown card is now a genuine error because 038 has run. `070-approve-intro-outro-human` is **absorbed into 037 and deleted** — it was this gate scoped to the zones only, which left the body's build-vs-reuse call unmade by anyone. Human gate count unchanged at three (037, 080, 120).
  - **Three ways to make a card became one.** `bespoke` is gone from `resolve.mjs`, `render.mjs`, `PIPELINE.md` and the audit prompt, and `scripts/promote-bespoke.mjs` is deleted (it copied a bespoke dir into the catalog and printed a stub, was never wired into any step, and its input no longer exists). Owner ruling: one shared collection, every new card goes straight into it.
  - **Ordering fix over patching:** because 038 runs before 040, `resolveCues` never meets an unbuilt card, so the line-order bug is unreachable rather than worked around. `validateCues` stays deliberately tolerant of unbuilt cards — it is what `run.sh <slug> validate` runs before 037, alongside a new catalog-independent `validateAnchors`, so the owner never approves a plan whose anchors cannot resolve.
  - Feedback keys carry the routing: `zone-intro:*` / `zone-conclusion:*` → the 035 rulebook, `card-body:*` → the 030 rulebook, never across (preserves the 2026-07-29 separation). `card-plan.json` approval resets whenever the plan changes — including when 038 builds a card and its status flips `new` → `existing`, which is the only moment the built card is compared against the approved spec.

2026-07-29 — **Intro/conclusion are authored by their own pass, with their own rulebook, numbers and feedback** (owner: "i want intro conclusion steps to be very explicit and not tied with full body.. its rules, guildeliness, execution should be seprate"): new step `035-place-intro-outro-llm` reading `lib/zone-rules.mjs` + `lib/zone-constants.mjs`; `030` becomes BODY ONLY (`R_ZONES` retired, replaced by `R_BODY_SCOPE`); cues carry a `zone` field that `W19` cross-checks against the measured span. The 070 gate gained per-card and per-zone note boxes writing `zone-*` items into `feedback.json`, and the 130 fold routes those to the zone rulebook ONLY — never the body's, and never the reverse. Cards stay ONE shared collection (owner explicitly rejected a zone-only family: "No need to restrict on templates"). **The load-bearing measurement: the rejected test-03 intro ran 3.57 cues/min against a 2.30/min body — it was DENSER than the body.** So the zone bar is motion, not count: `W16` enacted share (test-03: 1 of 4), `W15` gap 20s vs the body's 45s (test-03 had 45.7s), `W18` measures the footage itself via freezedetect (test-03: 12.4s and 9.4s static runs), and `W17` rate is only a regression floor that test-03's intro already passed.

2026-07-24 — **HeyGen Avatar III is free for testing — use it without per-run approval** (owner: "heygen 3 does not cost money, whenever testing you can use heygen 3"): Avatar III via heygen-web (unlimited mode) may be submitted by sessions for TEST renders without asking each time; metered features (Avatar IV / generative credits) stay owner-gated. Confirms and extends the 2026-07-16 unlimited-meter finding. Production submits still announced, never from cron.

2026-07-24 — **Template-notes loop live + first application** (owner notes in the card gallery): deleted 5 more cards by owner note — `overlay/arrow-label`, `overlay/spotlight-click`, `enacted/verdict-scale`, `enacted/price-meter`, `enacted/connect-nodes` ("not needed"); `comparison/table-rows` got color coding (accent header, zebra rows, `*good`/`!bad` cell pills the cue pass can set); `checklist/icon-pills` rows left-aligned (icons one line); `title/title-cinematic-float` title 88→112px. Library is now 59 cards. Also same-day: SFX kit was silent (samples peaked −27..−66 dBFS) — gen-sfx-kit.sh now peak-normalizes to −3 dBFS and build-mix hard-fails if a planned SFX bus peaks under −35 dB.

2026-07-24 — **Card library pruned + Ken Burns removed for good (owner, test-01 Final Cut round 1)**: (a) `enacted/stack-builder`, `overlay/label-plate`, and `overlay/callout` are DELETED (dir + catalog + QA list) — owner: "not going to use in video" / "remove this overlay from everywhere, not liking it"; callout's role is taken by the new `overlay/keyword-pop` (minimal corner pill, ≤5-word phrase + optional brand kicker) with number-moments on the contrast-fixed `stat-hit`, UI-pointing on `arrow-label`. (b) Ken Burns drift on screen segments removed FOREVER (`lib/effects/drift.mjs` deleted, `--drift` flag gone; regression-guarded by the "screen segments stay static" integration test) — owner: "what is this effect called? lets remove this forever, not adding much value". (c) New lint error E9 overlay-over-graphic: overlays may never overlap a fullframe card's extended span (owner saw callouts stacked on the title card and a tool-intro). (d) Cue routing folds: comma-enumerations go to list cards (icon-pills/bullet-points), never kinetic-sentence (R_KINETIC); `enacted/pipeline-flow` steps now REQUIRE an icon from the shared 28-icon inline-SVG set (Lucide-style, grown per need). (e) Title card `title-aurora-wave` reworked: logo+name chips, symmetric grid, motion fills the whole 10s hold (owner: "motion stopped at 2 sec").

2026-07-16 — founders-tracker: `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` and `wrangler` are EXACT-pinned as a matched pair (currently `1.45.0` + `4.111.0`) — never caret-range them and never bump one alone. The plugin generates `dist/founders_tracker/wrangler.json` and the wrangler CLI validates it, so a version gap between them breaks the deploy. This supersedes 8a737cd ("pin ... for Node 22 compat", which pinned plugin→1.39.0 but held wrangler at 3.114.17): that combination was already inverted, since plugin 1.39.0's own peer dep asks for `wrangler ^4.95.0`. The mismatch is what produced the deploy-time "Unexpected fields" warnings — v3 wrangler couldn't parse the v4-schema config the plugin emitted. Node-22 compat is a non-issue: wrangler 4 declares `engines.node >=22`, and the box is on v22.14.0. Upgrading wrangler alone then fails harder, not softer — 4.111 rejects the `legacy_env` key that plugin 1.39 still injects (a hard ERROR, so `wrangler deploy --dry-run` is the cheap way to catch this before it hits prod). Upgrading the pair also pulled `@cloudflare/workers-types` to v5 (wrangler 4's peerOptional) and cleared 6 npm audit findings (5 high). Vite stays on 6.x — plugin 1.45 still accepts `^6.1.0`.

2026-07-16 — heygen-web `generate-from-audio` only drives Talking Photos (`photo_avatar`), NOT `instant_avatar` looks. Root-caused from a web-UI HAR (`~/Downloads/app.heygen.com16.har`) after avatar `f64bdab33dcf4136b32d66da2a74ed28` rendered fine in the browser but failed `photar_not_found` via the CLI. The CLI's `generate-audio-save/generate.json` payloads hardcode `avatar_type: "photo_avatar"` + `photar_version: "V3"`; HeyGen then looks the id up in the Talking-Photo table and 404s. The web UI's `/v1/text_draft.save` `content` block for an instant_avatar is `{ avatar_id, avatar_state_id (both = look id), render_type: "normal", avatar_type: "instant_avatar" }`, avatar group `80a93446fd274f83b6ac72e5fcc6b10c`. So the failure is an avatar-TYPE mismatch, not "not owned". To support instant avatars, add a second payload path keyed off avatar_type (needs the full instant_avatar save+generate payloads captured byte-for-byte from a HAR, per the CLI's payload convention). Note: the HAR was truncated/corrupt (~char 28M) so it can't be JSON-parsed whole — scan the raw text. Related: [[the photar_not_found-at-render-time note in RENDERS.md]].

2026-07-16 — HeyGen share-link slug uses a DOUBLE dash before the video_id. The canonical URL is `https://app.heygen.com/videos/<slugify(title)>--<video_id>` — a single dash 404s ("This video doesn't exist"). Verified against a real render: `dustin-11e82ae7df844be8a5695ee864e44f49--e9c7314895eb4b31a591596b6efb33f7`. The HeyGen API returns only the CDN mp4 url (`video_url` = files2.heygen.ai…), never the app share URL, so the link must be constructed — hence `heygenLink()` in `tooling/cli/heygen-web/src/cli/render-log.mjs`. Earlier single-dash links (from two casually-pasted old examples) were all wrong and were bulk-fixed in RENDERS.md. HTTP probing can't validate these (app.heygen.com is a SPA that returns 200 + renders 404 client-side; unauth curls hit a 401 redirect), so trust the rule, not a curl status.

2026-07-16 — HeyGen Avatar III unlimited plan is BACK, and the `heygen-web` "~20 min/month cap" was a misread meter. Proven on the owner's Creator plan against a real render (look `6c5ff54cb83e4284b0119598c058d3b2`, girl-1 intro audio, 1920×1080/86.6s): `usage --save`→submit→`usage --diff` returned `credits +0, seconds +0` (only a free priority slot +1/100 moved). The `/v1/avatar/video_generate/limits` `total_limit: 1200s` pool the CLI reported as an Avatar III cap is actually the **generative/free-credit** meter (Avatar IV + AI generative features), which unlimited-mode Avatar III (`use_unlimited_mode: true`) does not touch. Fixed the wording in `tooling/cli/heygen-web/{README,CLAUDE}.md`; the ✓UNLIMITED / ⚠️NOT-free `usage --diff` guard is the way to confirm any op is free. Updates the 2026-07-12 "$1/min HeyGen" cost assumption — Avatar III from the web session is free/unlimited, so the fal-lipsync un-deferral's cost lever is weaker than the notes imply (revisit `final-workflow-notes.md` economics). Also confirmed `generate-from-audio` works on owned Talking Photos (that look id is one).

2026-07-12 — Skill-library hardening pass (owner-directed): the `.claude/skills/` repo-operating library is now 21 skills — added `video-and-tts-reference` (domain-theory pack: voice cloning, sync math, avatar/GPU economics, Whisper — the video peer of `cloudflare-and-vps-reference`), brought `orchestrate`/`audit-repo-route`/`plan-review` up to the house convention (trigger-budgeted descriptions, When-NOT-to-use, dated provenance), consolidated the proof-and-analysis recipes + claim-evidence bar into `personal-stuff-research-methodology` (external-positioning slot deliberately skipped — private money repo, nothing published), replaced every "project memory" evidence pointer with in-repo anchors or honest embedded records, and shipped executable guards inside skills: `check-descriptions.sh` (diagnostics skill; now also wired into `scripts/relink.sh` so the ≤500/700 budget covers `.claude/skills/` too), `verify-inventory.sh` (hosting-inventory), `verify.sh` (config-and-secrets, build-and-env). QA'd by 3 independent reviewers (factual/doctrine/usability) + a fixer; the one blocking catch: a skill had invented "HeyGen Avatar IV is the path" — the operational rule stays Avatar III via heygen-web, never `--iv`/metered MCP.
