- 2026-07-28 (card-library / visuals-flow, boss) — **A card plan's `test_cmd` must cover every package that CONSUMES catalog.json, not just card-library's own gate; `check-catalog.mjs` now enforces the beat-card field contract.** Plan 157 added `enacted/bad-clip-montage` with `kind: "beat"` but no `max_beats`/`max_reveal_chars`. Its `test_cmd` was `card-library && bash scripts/check-cards.sh`, which passed — that gate validates folder shape, registration, side/type-scale contracts and logos, but knows nothing about how visuals-flow reads the catalog. visuals-flow's `synthCalibrationVars` computes `const maxBeats = card.max_beats ?? 0`, so the card silently synthesized ZERO beats and `lib/board.test.mjs`'s "every beat card synthesizes exactly max_beats beats" failed with `expected undefined beats (0 !== undefined)`. Net effect: PR#115 landed green on its own gate and left **main red**, which then parked the NEXT merge (PR#113 / plan 155) on a failure that had nothing to do with it — the misleading-attribution cost is the real damage, not the two missing fields. Fixes: (a) the entry gets `max_beats: 3` (plan 157 mandates three failure states, beat frames verified at 1.5/4.5/7.5s) and `max_reveal_chars: 28` (matches `checklist/icon-pills`; the card's label spec caps at `max_words: 4`); (b) `check-catalog.mjs` now fails when a `beat`/`word-sync` card omits either field, and when a non-reveal card declares them — so this class of break is caught in card-library where the author sees it. Calibrated against the catalog rather than assumed: all 22 `beat` + the 1 `word-sync` card declare both, 0 of 38 `single` do; a first cut asserting "only kind==beat" wrongly failed `slate/kinetic-sentence`. STILL OPEN (owner call, not boss's): `enacted/bad-clip-montage` is the only one of 22 beat cards with no `beat_shape` — it drives its three clips through a `variables.clips` array, so its beats carry timing but no content fields. The two fields make the suite pass because the test tolerates `{at}`-only beats; bringing the card onto the beat contract every sibling uses means rewriting its HTML.
- 2026-07-28 (visuals-flow, session) — **The exclusion zone and overlay-on-footage rules are BOTH kept; plan 158 was mis-specified, and E7 is avatar-blind.** Plan 158's crew reported that on a 79.2s `base:"none"` conclusion workdir, `E4 exclusion-zones` and `E9 overlay-over-graphic` are mutually unsatisfiable with mandatory end CTAs, and proposed exempting `base:"none"` from exclusion zones, allowing overlays over cards, or capping absorption. **All three are rejected.** The rules are correct and the conflict is real but self-inflicted: `base:"none"` means *there is no footage at all* — `fillGapsWithFreeze` turns every screen segment into a FREEZE FRAME, which is exactly why `E7 uncovered-second` demands that fullframe cards cover every active second. Plan 158 set `base:"none"` **and never ran the avatar step**, so the conclusion genuinely had no presenter footage; a video that must be 100% graphics, on an 80s runtime with a 20s end-exclusion zone and two mandatory end overlays, is correctly impossible. The fix is footage, not a weaker rule: the conclusion is a talking head and must carry avatar spans, after which cards cover only what they should, overlays sit on avatar footage, and both rules are satisfiable. (Note the CTA slugs were never the E4 problem — `ENDCARD_SLUG_PREFIXES = ['brand/','link-in-description/','like-subscribe/']` already exempts them; E4 fired on a *verdict* card stretched to the video end.) Setting `base:"screen"` to escape this is explicitly rejected — it reintroduces the 300s screen-recording cap that plan 158 exists to remove. **Separately, a genuine latent bug was found while judging this:** `lib/lint-cues.mjs` contains ZERO references to avatar, so E7 counts only fullframe cards as coverage and would demand full card coverage even on a `base:"none"` video whose avatar spans already fill the screen; `extendExposure`'s `base === 'none'` branch likewise absorbs the whole gap to the next fullframe with no awareness that an avatar segment sits in it — burying the presenter, which is precisely what W12 (plan 156) exists to catch. Both are the pipeline's recurring shape: *computed on one surface, never consumed on the next* (fourth instance this week, after the chroma plate, the exposure regression, and the discarded three-file structure). Plan 162 fixes both; plan 158 is re-planned on top of it.
- 2026-07-28 (plans, session) — **A plan's `test_cmd` must be able to FAIL on that plan's own deliverable.** Plan 158's gate was `bash scripts/check.sh`, which reported 314/314 green while the video workdir the plan produced failed its own `lint-cues`. A repo-wide suite that cannot see the deliverable is not a merge gate. `plans/_TEMPLATE.md` now says so and gives the video-workdir form (`... && node lib/lint-cues.mjs <slug>`). This generalizes the same lesson as the 2026-07-28 boss entry above (a card plan's gate must cover every package that consumes catalog.json) and as LESSONS 2026-07-24 (plan 140's UI behaviours passed a gate that could not observe them).
- 2026-07-28 (visuals-flow, session) — **`EXPOSURE_TAIL` retired; its rule text moved onto `MAX_FULLFRAME_ONSCREEN`.** Plan 155 stopped adding the 0.4s pad (a tail past a sentence boundary lands inside the NEXT sentence in contiguous speech — precisely the defect it was meant to prevent), which left a constant with ZERO live consumers. Plan 155 had assumed "other code may read it"; a grep proved otherwise. It was not dead weight though: `build-prompt.mjs` injects every constant's `rule` string into the cue-pass prompt, so deleting it outright would have silently dropped real guidance from what the model reads. The sentence now rides on `MAX_FULLFRAME_ONSCREEN`, a number the resolver actually consumes, so the guidance stays attached to something live. General rule this encodes: a constant in `cue-constants.mjs` is two things at once — a value for code and a sentence for the prompt — and retiring one half means relocating the other, never dropping it.
- 2026-07-29 (visuals-flow, session) — **The avatar clamp keys on a span's END, not its start; boundary matrix added.** Plan 158 was blocked three times by three distinct bugs in one code path, each visible only once the previous was fixed (E7 avatar-blindness → E9 avatar-blindness → this). The third: `extendExposure`'s clamp used `avatarSpans.find(([s]) => s > end + 0.001)`, so a span starting EXACTLY at a card's natural end was skipped — and exactly-adjacent is the NORMAL arrangement, because resolve places the avatar span right where the card ends. On the conclusion workdir c02's end and the avatar start were both 49.28, the clamp was skipped, the card absorbed the full 20s `HOLD_EXTEND_CAP` to 69.28, and E9 then reported the two MANDATORY end CTAs as overlapping it. A 0.02s input change swung the result by 20 seconds. Fixed by finding the first span not yet FINISHED (`([, e]) => e > end + EPS`) and clamping to `max(spanStart, end)`, which resolves all four boundary relationships in one predicate: span exactly at the end (no extension), span already running at the end (no extension — never push further into a presenter the card already touches), span later (clamp to its start), no span (unchanged). **The second bug was found by the test matrix, not by the report**: writing cases for all four relationships immediately failed the "span already running at the end" case, which nobody had reported. Lesson recorded for this path: a single-case fix here has now failed three times — cover the whole boundary matrix, not the case that happened to be observed.
- 2026-07-29 (visuals-flow, session) — **`base:"none"` now assembles end to end; three defects fixed and the missed-call-site CLASS closed with a test.** Plan 158 was blocked four times, each by a different bug in the same area. (1) `render.mjs` and `assemble.mjs` recomputed exposure for their staleness guard WITHOUT `avatarSpans` while `resolved.json` was written WITH it, so `JSON.stringify(recomputed) === JSON.stringify(resolved)` could never match and both refused to run without `--force` on **any** video carrying avatar-full spans — a repo-wide regression introduced by the 162/166 series. (2) `assemble.mjs` required `screen.mp4` unconditionally with no `manifest.base` check, so a `base:"none"` video could never assemble; the only way past was a dummy file. (3) `-frames:v 1` (an OUTPUT option) was spliced before `-i` for the head-freeze case — ffmpeg rejects it with "Option frames:v cannot be applied to input url". Latent on `base:"screen"` because that path never freezes; `base:"none"` fills gaps with freezes and exposed it. Fixed by mirroring the existing tail idiom: read a 0.05s sliver from the start (`-ss 0 -t 0.05`, both genuine input options) and let the existing `tpad=stop_mode=clone` hold it — works in both the `filter_complex` and plain `-vf` branches. **The durable part is the fourth change**: a test asserting EVERY `extendExposure` call site in `lib/` passes `avatarSpans`, proven to fail by removing one. Three consecutive plans were blocked by a *different* missed call site (E7, then E9, then these two), each fix targeting only the site that had been reported — the class needed a property test, not a fourth point fix. Verified end to end: the 79.2s `base:"none"` conclusion renders and assembles, with both end CTAs compositing over footage.
- 2026-07-30 (visuals-flow, owner) — **The concept pass's 80% narration-coverage rule is now a machine gate, and the metric it gates on is the one that renders.** The rule ("spans must cover at least 80% of narration") had lived only as prose in `steps/020-choose-concept-llm/concept-pass-prompt.md` and was enforced by nobody: `lint-concept.mjs` checked required fields and anchor resolution but had no coverage check, so a concept at 77.8% passed clean. Found while explaining the metric to the owner — the honest answer was "that number clears no gate". Now `MIN_NARRATION_COVERAGE = 0.8` in `lib/lint-concept.mjs`, with `narrationCoverage()` exported and the figure printed on success. **Three details are load-bearing.** (1) The denominator is `kind: "narration"` segments only, read from `segments.json` (now a required input to the gate — a missing file is a hard error, since 015 always precedes 020). Demo and playback stretches are excluded because only overlay pills are legal there and a pill carries no full-frame mood, so a register over them would mean nothing. (2) Coverage is measured with `concept-spans.mjs`'s span geometry, extracted into a new exported `spansFromRegisters()` that `loadConceptSpans()` now delegates to — so the gated figure describes the SAME spans that drive the `whip-reg` transitions rather than a second measurement free to drift from them. This re-measured the in-flight `opusclip-vs-submagic` from 93.2% (hand-computed off each anchor's last-word `end`) to 91.4% (the real geometry: `to_anchor` first word + 1.0s); the lower number is the correct one. (3) The check runs only when no other error fired, because an unresolved anchor drops its span and would report a coverage shortfall that is really a typo. **The gate is deliberately gameable and says so.** Merging two adjacent spans of the SAME register raises the percentage without changing a frame — same-register spans skin cards identically and produce no flip between them. The error text, the prompt and the step README all now say to extend a span over uncovered narration instead. **Why coverage matters at all:** a cue in an uncovered stretch inherits no register, so its card falls back to its own default look and `E8 concept-register` has no span to check it against — the same failure class as the 2026-07-24 bug where register never reached `VARS` and every card rendered its default. Both existing videos pass unchanged (`opusclip-vs-submagic` 91.4%, `test-01` 97.0%), so no rework; 430 tests green. **Process note:** this edits a lint constant mid-run, which guardrail 6 of the `visuals-flow` skill routes through the 130 fold instead. Owner asked for it directly and the in-flight video passes the new gate untouched, so nothing was invalidated — but the guardrail is the normal path and this was the exception.
- 2026-07-30 (visuals-flow, owner) — **A session step now files itself as `running` when its prompt is handed out; a forgotten `done` announces itself instead of reading as clean.** The run ledger already had a five-state lifecycle and `run.sh`'s `record_step` wrapper bracketed every SCRIPT step correctly (`running` before the command, `done` with warnings scraped from its own output after), and the board recorded the gates — including a `running` entry for 080 saying "waiting on the owner". Session steps (020/030/035/050/060/130) were the hole: `run.sh` prints a prompt and exits, so there is no process for `record_step` to wrap and no code path that could ever mark them in flight. `running` is also structurally un-inferrable — `ARTIFACT_PROOF` decides by looking for a step's OUTPUT, and a step in flight has not produced one, so an unrecorded running step is indistinguishable from an unstarted one. That left the state depending on the model remembering, which failed twice in one session: 038 showed `todo` while being built, and five completed steps showed `done (inferred from artifacts, no summary recorded)` with no record of what they did or found. **Fix, in the shape the file already used:** each session verb calls `record <num> running` as its first statement, so the harness owns the transition INTO running and the actor owns the transition out — the same split as `record_step` and as the board's 080 entry. A session that asks for a prompt and abandons it correctly sits `running` (it was started and never finished; `blocked` exists for explicit failure). **Second half:** `done` stays session-written because only the session knows what it did, so `renderTable` promotes the `derived` count from a parenthetical into a `WARNING` naming the steps and the backfill command — a forgotten entry is now visible rather than passing as done. **A guard came with it:** `resolveWorkdir(".")` is the pipeline root and `scripts/test-run-sh.sh` drives verbs with slug `.`, so the new writes would have dropped a `run-log.json` beside `run.sh` that records nothing and is not gitignored; `run-log.mjs` now refuses a WRITE whose target resolves to the pipeline root while still allowing the READ that `run.sh . status` performs. Explicitly rejected: inferring `running` from artifacts (impossible), and blocking progress on a missing entry (turns a bookkeeping miss into a hard stop). Tests: 4 new (root-write refusal, warning surfaced, warning absent when recorded, plus run.sh asserting each session verb records and that slug `.` writes nothing); the pre-existing `/1 inferred/` assertion was updated to the new wording. Full `scripts/check.sh` green. Same mid-run caveat as the coverage gate: guardrail 6 routes rule changes through the 130 fold, and the owner asked for this directly.

- **2026-07-31 — Browser-UI standard: components-first, template strings only for trivial reports.**
  Any browser UI in this repo with more than one view/route, any editing state
  that saves, or realistic growth beyond a few hundred lines is built as a
  **Vite + React + TS component app** (one component per concern, co-located
  CSS on the existing CSS vars, `dist/` gitignored, served by its owning
  backend). Exemplars: `apps/tutorial-tracker-app`,
  `pipelines/video/visuals-flow/board-ui`. Hand-rolled HTML/CSS/JS template
  strings remain acceptable only for trivial single-view static reports
  (contact sheets, one-shot HTML dumps). **Why:** the visuals-flow review board grew to
  2900 lines of server-emitted template strings over ~15 incremental plans —
  nothing ever said "stop and componentize", every plan correctly imitated the
  surrounding idiom, and the endpoint was triple-defined controls, page-scoped
  CSS bugs, and a full rewrite (plans 169–174). The existing UI skills
  (ui-craft, frontend-design) govern visual quality, not architecture; this
  decision is the architecture rule they sit on top of. Recorded in the
  personal-stuff-architecture-contract skill's invariants table. Existing
  hand-rolled tools (media-board, pinterest-board, card-library local gallery)
  are grandfathered — migrate opportunistically when next materially touched,
  not as a big-bang.

- **2026-07-31 — `yt-script-2`: scripting from owner-supplied knowledge, built clean
  rather than by reworking `yt-script`.**
  Owner wants one simple path — he hands over a video title plus whatever knowledge
  he has, gets an outline, approves it, and asks for the script if he wants one.
  Three earlier shapes were considered in-session and rejected by him as too
  complicated: extending `yt-script`, reusing its `Guidelines/voice.md`, and wiring
  in `dossiers/`. His words: *"nah man too complicated... keep it simple. no
  dossier/research/voice flow."*
  **Why a second folder instead of fixing the first:** `yt-script/`'s
  `structure.md` (234 lines) is a tier-list comparison format — four tiers,
  ranked Winners, pricing from screenshots, a "link in the description" beat per
  tool. That is a *format*, not a *framework*, so generalizing it would have meant
  deleting most of it. It ran once (`scripts/n8n-hosting/`) and stays as-is for
  comparison videos. The two share no files and neither replaces the other.
  **The load-bearing constraint:** `OUTLINE-INSTRUCTIONS.md` and
  `SCRIPT-INSTRUCTIONS.md` are owner-owned and start as explicit placeholders. The
  skill must STOP and ask when it hits one — improvising a format produces output
  that looks right and is wrong, which is the expensive failure here. Both the
  SKILL.md and the folder CLAUDE.md name this as the trap.
  **Context worth keeping:** `docs/yt-research-v2-brainstorm-handoff.md` (2026-07-07)
  recorded the same complaints about the old pipeline — too much manual input,
  weak output, cost-heavy. Its recommended approach #1 (persistent per-tool
  dossiers) was built as `pipelines/youtube/dossiers/`; this decision covers the
  *scripting* side, which nothing had replaced. `yt-research/` is now referenced by
  neither script path — dormant since 2026-07-04, 3.8 MB, one niche. Kept, not
  deleted: its TS pipeline is the only working YouTube transcript+pricing scraper
  in the repo.

- **2026-07-31 — `yt-script-2` outline format: lane blocks in markdown, delivered
  as a dark PDF. Markdown is the source, never the deliverable.**
  The owner supplied a real working outline (the HeyGen 5-tool avatar comparison)
  and the problem with it: *"its currently very jumbled, not easy to grasp."*
  Diagnosis held four findings — the same instruction appeared as `Notes`,
  `Editor Notes`, `(Editor: …)` and a bare parenthetical; verbatim voiceover and
  directions sat at the same indent with nothing distinguishing them; the rule
  that breaks the video if missed ("never play a finished output in this
  section") was an unlabelled paragraph; and that rule was then restated in all
  five per-tool beats, where repetition turned it into wallpaper.
  **The format:** every beat carries `SAY` / `SHOW` / `EDIT` in a fixed left
  column; section-wide rules hoist into one red box; spoken copy is the only
  blockquote in the document, so the indent itself says "read this aloud". Four
  layouts were mocked up and compared; lane blocks won on the two criteria the
  owner named — grasp speed, and holding up once beats grow long.
  **Delivery — three formats were tried and two rejected by the owner.** Google
  Docs twice: v1 flattened because paragraph-level labels collapse on HTML
  import, v2 rebuilt with per-beat two-column tables and was still rejected
  ("readability is very bad"). Raw markdown rejected as a deliverable — it reads
  as markup to a non-technical freelancer. Settled on markdown as source →
  `render-outline.mjs` → HTML → PDF, because the maker only *reads* the outline
  (he copies the body into his own doc to draft script), so editability was never
  a real constraint.
  **Three print-CSS details are load-bearing and all fail silently.**
  (1) `print-color-adjust: exact` — browsers strip backgrounds when printing, and
  without it every lane chip and rules box prints as an identical grey rectangle;
  the colour coding *is* the layout, so this is not cosmetic. (2) `@page{margin:0}`
  with padding moved onto `.wrap` — a normal page margin stops the dark ground at
  the text area and frames every page in white. (3) Parts deliberately do NOT
  force a page break; they did, and it left pages ~70% empty in a document that
  is scrolled rather than bound.
  **The standing trap:** `render-outline.mjs` is a parser, not a formatter. A
  lane label not alone on its own line, or spoken copy outside a blockquote,
  renders as plain prose with no lane — no error, no warning, just a worse
  outline that still looks fine at a glance. OUTLINE-INSTRUCTIONS.md carries the
  exact recognised forms as a table. A lint for this was discussed and deferred.
  Generated `outline.html` / `outline.pdf` are gitignored per the house rule.
- 2026-08-01 — **visuals-flow kickoff config (step 005) + express mode + Drive delivery (step 150).** The owner picks per video, once, at kickoff: engine (`heygen3` free default | `heygen4` metered — writing it IS the authorization for that video's avatar batches) and review mode (`full` default | `express`). Express waives the 037/080 board approvals in the code gates themselves and runs unattended to the final cut; it NEVER waives the new-card look-preview ("even if I say run till final cut, if you are making new motion graphics I still want the prompt") nor the 120 final-cut gate. This amends "none is skippable at any video length" (2026-07-29): the owner's own recorded kickoff choice is the one sanctioned bypass; a session still never skips a gate by its own judgment. Delivery: step 150 uploads the approved full-res `final.mp4` to the video's own Drive folder (`Output/` subfolder, `pp-drive --overwrite`), destination in `run-config.json` (`drive_folder`/`drive_account`).
- 2026-08-01 — **vf2 kickoff config (step 005) + express mode + Drive delivery (step 150).** The owner picks per video, once, at kickoff: engine (`heygen3` free default | `heygen4` metered — writing it IS the authorization for that video's avatar batches) and review mode (`full` default | `express`). Express waives the 037/080 board approvals in the code gates themselves and runs unattended to the final cut; it NEVER waives the new-card look-preview ("even if I say run till final cut, if you are making new motion graphics I still want the prompt") nor the 120 final-cut gate. This amends "none is skippable at any video length" (2026-07-29): the owner's own recorded kickoff choice is the one sanctioned bypass; a session still never skips a gate by its own judgment. Delivery: step 150 uploads the approved full-res `final.mp4` to the video's own Drive folder (`Output/` subfolder, `pp-drive --overwrite`), destination in `run-config.json` (`drive_folder`/`drive_account`).
- 2026-08-02 — **intro-studio reviews the HTML, not the render; and owner feedback becomes rules, not chat.** Three findings forced this. (1) `hyperframes check` was reporting `layout: ok` on the film with **zero samples** — a `../` asset path is a lint ERROR, and when lint errors the layout, motion and contrast passes never sample. The film's media lived one level up in the workdir, so every mechanical check on this project had been vacuous. `lib/film-assets.mjs` links the media into `film/assets/` (gitignored) and the composition references `assets/<name>`; with lint clean the same check samples 498 times and finds real occlusions, including six agenda lines running under logo tiles — which is exactly the owner's "text is overlapping with some motion graphic". (2) **Render is now the LAST step, not the review step.** `run.sh <slug> review` runs check plus a beat-midpoint snapshot pass in ~2 minutes with no encode; every visual defect this pipeline has produced was visible in a still, and reviewing the mp4 cost minutes per look. `review/REVIEW.md` pairs each beat frame with the `stage` line it must satisfy, because the defects that matter most (a crown landing on the presenter) render as a perfectly clean frame. (3) AUTHORING.md pointed the author at `brand.json` — five colour tokens, no typography — which is the root cause of the first film's 54px Helvetica look. It now requires reading `card-library/DESIGN.md` in full and the logo registry; card-library stays **read-only** from here, and the no-templates rule is unchanged (a design system is the brand, not a template). New `TASTE.md` holds owner reactions as numbered rules, each recording whether a machine enforces it or it is author judgement; `check-film-style.mjs` mechanizes the type contract and flags any beat with no motion of its own. `scripts/check.sh` switched from a hand-kept test list to a glob, because an enumerated gate silently skips every test added after it was written.
- 2026-08-02 — **boss: the mutation gate is machine-run, not requested in prose (batch retro).** Across a nine-PR batch, *zero* crews produced the mutation evidence their briefs demanded, and two shipped gates that could not fire: PR#134's new test read `render.mjs` as **source text** and regex-matched the exact line its mutation commented out (circular — the only failing test of 27), and PR#137's `E14` never fired at all (the plan's own mutation produced zero E-codes, exit 0). **Both passed `test_cmd`.** Asking for evidence is unenforceable; running the mutation is not. New frontmatter `mutation_apply` / `mutation_command` / `mutation_expect` (+ optional `mutation_cwd`, `mutation_timeout`) arms `boss-merge` to prove it: clean must pass → apply → must FAIL printing the expected marker → revert → pass again. It also rejects a no-op recipe, a marker already present on clean, and crash-shaped failures (`SyntaxError`, `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`…) where the marker is only echoed source text. Corollary found the same day: **plan 175's own mutation recipe was wrong** — a 14-word title tripped a pre-existing `max_words 7` rule before the frame gate ran — and nobody noticed because nothing ever executed it. Recipes must be dry-run during recon and written against data, never by disabling the rule.
- 2026-08-02 — **boss `ui:true` screenshot gate REINSTATED as a real merge gate.** Reverses the 2026-07-18 removal. That removal was correct about the *mechanism* — it was only a crew-brief instruction, enforced by nobody and consumed by nobody, so it was pure cost. What changed is consumption: the owner rejected PR#141 outright for landing without its storyboard screenshot ("do not accept it without both"). Re-added in the form the original lacked — `boss-merge` REJECTS a `ui: true` branch that commits no image, rather than asking the crew nicely. Set `ui: true` only when a screenshot is genuinely wanted.
- 2026-08-02 — **Standing crew rules moved from per-batch `--brief-extra` into the dispatch brief template.** Learned by repetition in one batch: crews backgrounded their gate and then idled ("I am waiting for the background task to finish") on **both** executors, four times — `claude -p` and agy cannot wait on a background job, so the run burns doing nothing. Also now standing: commit early (one crew hit the turn cap with 11 modified files and zero commits, losing everything); never reserialise a data file (crews re-emitted whole `cues.json`/`index.html` with a formatter, turning 3–20 line changes into 2000-line whole-file conflicts on PR#135 and PR#137); leave the worktree clean; never commit regenerated artifacts (`run-log.json` caused two separate rebase conflicts).
- 2026-08-02 — **Deterministic pre-merge gates beat brief prose.** Every rule below was already written in the crew brief and violated anyway, so each became code in `boss-merge`: rejects edits to `plans/README.md` (PR#138 committed it despite dispatch force-resetting it), scratch/junk files (`scratch*.mjs`, `*.pid`, `*.mp4`, `before-*.txt` — PR#136's one-off `scratch.mjs` actually reached main), regenerated artifacts, and missing `ui:true` evidence. General principle for boss: **if a rule matters, make it a gate; a rule that only lives in the brief will be broken.**
- 2026-08-02 — **boss failure taxonomy: `ratelimited` ≠ `blocked`.** An API 429 ("You've hit your session limit") killed two crews mid-flight and `collect` reported it as "blocked … (max-turns or execution error)" — a wrong diagnosis that points at the plan when the cause is the clock, and that would spend the single fix-up round on a self-clearing condition. `claude-p` now classifies RATELIMIT / MAXTURNS / APIERROR / ERROR from the JSON envelope, and both executors report **uncommitted work** in the worktree (a mid-flight kill often leaves a complete implementation uncommitted — salvage it with a DIRECT executor dispatch, never `boss-dispatch`, which force-resets the branch). agy's terminal `ERROR` with HEAD advanced and a clean tree is now reported as **done**: agy emits cosmetic scheduler faults after committing, which nearly triggered a pointless fix-up on PR#141.
- 2026-08-02 — **Concurrent sessions are a first-class hazard for boss.** A second Claude session worked in the repo all afternoon and cost more wall-clock than any crew failure: merges parked repeatedly on a dirty main, one file (`tool-glass-tile/index.html`) re-conflicted three times because each resolution went stale before greenlight could land, and it landed two main-breaking regressions (an `E8` register error, then a `W5` first-beat-idle — both classified as hard errors) that blocked an unrelated PR for hours. Boss auto-commits a dirty main, so **it will commit another session's in-progress work under boss's name**. `boss-session-start` now warns when other `claude`/`agy` processes are running. Deeper fix (NOT done, worth doing): greenlight already leases a worktree — if it rebased/verified/pushed entirely inside it and never touched `REPO_TOPLEVEL`, the whole dirty-main class (parks, auto-commit hazard, sweeping others' work) would disappear.
- 2026-08-02 — **Chrome is a shared, serialized resource on the merge path.** Every visuals-flow/card-library `test_cmd` drives headless Chrome (`board-ui-smoke`, `card-qa`, `frame-gate`). PR#134's merge failed on `Chrome dump-dom timeout on #card-plan` with 44 chrome processes live because a crew was rendering concurrently. `boss-merge` now waits for live crews and holds `state/locks/chrome.lock` across the verify (`BOSS_CHROME_WAIT_MIN`, default 45). Related: `boss-merge` auto-frees any *clean* worktree still holding the target branch — three merges in one batch parked on "already used by worktree" while a finished crew's worktree held the ref — and refuses to touch a dirty one so uncommitted work is never stranded.
- 2026-08-02 — **`needs:` is prose; `needs_prs:` is a contract.** The intro-studio chain (180→181→182) was sequenced entirely by the owner telling boss when to dispatch, because `needs` is free text boss cannot act on. New `needs_prs: [138]` makes dispatch refuse until each listed PR is closed. New `touches: [lib/x.mjs]` makes boss warn at dispatch when an in-flight PR shares a file, instead of the collision surfacing as a merge conflict (three plans appended to `lib/lint-cues.mjs` in one batch and collided on the 2nd and 3rd merges).
- 2026-08-02 — **greenlight lands from inside its worktree; the "main checkout busy" park is gone.** Landing used to `merge --no-ff` + `push` in `REPO_TOPLEVEL`, which required that checkout to be clean AND on main. One unrelated uncommitted file therefore parked EVERY merge, and it was the single most expensive failure mode of the 2026-08-02 batch: a second Claude session editing the repo parked merges all afternoon, and it pushed boss into auto-committing that session's work-in-progress (under boss's name) purely to unblock itself. The worktree greenlight already leases is checked out, rebased and verified, so it now does the whole thing: detach at freshly-fetched `origin/main` → `merge --no-ff <branch>` → `push HEAD:main`, retrying from a new `origin/main` if the push is rejected (`GREENLIGHT_LAND_ATTEMPTS`, default 3) and parking rather than forcing. The top-level checkout is fast-forwarded **best-effort only** and is never a precondition; when it is dirty or off-main it is left strictly untouched and just ends up behind. Consequence for callers: anything committing to main after a land must `fetch` + `merge --ff-only` first instead of assuming its checkout is current — `boss-merge` now does this before appending its plan-registry row. Covered by two new tests: (h) dirty top-level still lands, the merge reaches `origin/main`, and the concurrent session's uncommitted edit survives byte-intact; (i) a top-level parked on another branch still lands and is not switched. Both were negative-controlled against the old guard to prove they are not vacuous.
- 2026-08-02 — **The Resolve export is a SECOND implementation of the video, and it had silently diverged from `assemble` in four places.** Step 140 had never actually been run on a video until this session; its first real use surfaced four independent defects, every one of them the same shape — a rule enforced in `assemble.mjs`/`build-mix.mjs` and simply absent from `export-timeline.mjs`. (1) `--bundle` wrote `src="./media/x.mp4"` into the FCPXML; `src` is a URL and Resolve resolves no relative ones, so the import reported **"97 of 97 clips were not yet found"** — the non-bundled branch had always been correct, which is why nothing caught it, and every test stubbed the URL builder as `srcUrl: (f) => f`. (2) SFX were referenced as raw `assets/sfx/*.wav` at unity, dropping `gainDb` (108 instances, 14–30 dB hot), `semi` (26 pitch-shifted), and the `-stream_loop` fill (`drone_low.wav` is 8s of media behind an 86.7s clip), while the VO lane was a raw `vo.mp3` with none of `VO_CHAIN` — the owner heard it immediately as "bg sound is too loud in start… voice over overall low volume". (3) The sidecar SRT shipped all 1120 caption chunks though the burn skips every segment whose kind is not `screen`, so captions appeared over the intro motion graphics. (4) Resolve anchors a subtitle clip to its FIRST cue, so an SRT starting at 25.61s played the whole track 25.6s early — the old unfiltered SRT had the same bug hidden behind a 0.4s first cue. Fixes 1–3 are structural: `sfxInstanceChain`/`VO_CHAIN` are now exported from `build-mix.mjs` and baked by both consumers, and caption scoping reuses `planSegments` rather than re-deriving it. Fix 4 is not a divergence but a format limit, handled with a zero-width anchor cue. **General principle: any second renderer of the same timeline will drift from the first; give the two one shared builder, or budget for the drift to be found by the owner rather than by a gate.** Also verified negatively: Resolve's subtitle importer REFUSES `.ass`, so per-word caption colour is unreachable in Resolve from any sidecar.
- 2026-08-03 — **`gh auth switch` is global state, and it put the work GitHub account on a public personal repo.** `~/.zshrc`'s `_gh_auto_switch` picked an account per directory and applied it with `gh auth switch`, which rewrites the active user in `~/.config/gh/hosts.yml` for the whole machine rather than for the calling shell. Two things then compounded. `.zshrc` is sourced only by *interactive* shells, and Claude Code and boss run `gh` from non-interactive ones, so the hook never ran for the tools that actually call `gh`; they simply inherited whatever account the last interactive terminal had set. Sitting in `personal-stuff`, `gh api user` returned `kushal-zluri` three times running. So boss posted four PR comments (#126 on 07-30, #133 on 08-01) as the **work** account on a **public** repo, and because commenting subscribes you to a thread, GitHub mailed them to `kushal.b@zluri.com`. Commit history was never affected: `.git/config` sets `user.email` per repo, so every commit stayed `akshatparty17@gmail.com`. Cleanup: the two substantive comments were reposted from `akshat-git-jpg` before all four were deleted, and the work account was unsubscribed from both threads. Fix has two layers, because either alone still fails. `_gh_auto_switch` now exports `GH_TOKEN` (process-local, so concurrent shells in different repos stop fighting over one global setting), and `.claude/settings.local.json` pins `GH_TOKEN` for this repo so non-interactive tools get the right identity even though they never read `.zshrc`. **General principle: a per-directory identity that writes global state will leak across concurrent shells and into every non-interactive tool. Pin identity per process, and assume anything invoked by an agent never sourced your shell rc.**
- 2026-08-03 — **A work skill shipped its production credential as a checked-in `.env`, in a repo that is public.** `tooling/claude-skills/` is shared by the work and personal Claude accounts, but it lives in THIS repo, which is public. One skill stored the credential it needed as a tracked `references/*.env` and its `SKILL.md` advertised that as a feature ("no manual auth setup, the token is stored in ..."), so nothing ever read as wrong. Found while auditing repo visibility for an unrelated reason. A scan of all 3694 tracked files found exactly one other hit, a fake example inside a redaction-guide table, so this was not systemic. The git-side fix: the env file is untracked (the local copy stays, so the skill still runs), `tooling/claude-skills/*/references/*.env` is gitignored with an `.example` carve-out, the helper script exits 1 with setup instructions instead of failing obscurely, and the SKILL.md line is inverted into one-time-setup. **Rotating a credential is the only real remediation and git cannot do it — anything that has been public is burned no matter what history says afterwards.** Deliberately keeping this entry vague on which credential and which commit: this file is public too, and a precise writeup is a map. Details live outside the repo. **General principle: a skill in `tooling/claude-skills/` must never carry its own secret. Ship an `.example`, read the real value from an ignored path, and assume every word of this repo is world-readable.**
- 2026-08-04 — **The Skyscanner MCP is installed and registered, and it cannot return data from this IP.** `tooling/mcp/mcp-skyscanner/` ([shadyvb/mcp-skyscanner](https://github.com/shadyvb/mcp-skyscanner)) needs no API key because it drives a reverse-engineered Skyscanner *mobile* client, whose auth is a PerimeterX token the vendored lib forges locally (`vendor/skyscanner/skyscanner/px.py`). That is exactly why it fails: every call returns 403 `{"action":"captcha"}`, consistently across retries, and the vendored lib is already at upstream HEAD so the solver is not stale. Upstream's own README states the remedy is to switch IP and mint a fresh `px_authorization`, i.e. it only works behind a proxy, which `mcp_server.py` has no env var for. One local patch was needed to even see this: all four 403 handlers indexed `req.json()["redirect_to"]`, a key a captcha body does not carry, so every block surfaced as `KeyError: 'redirect_to'` and looked like a library bug rather than an IP ban. **General principle: when a scraper needs no API key, its auth is something it forges, and the forgery is the thing that will break. Budget for a proxy before adopting one, and check that its error paths can actually report a block.** What DOES work is the boring path: drive the normal website in a **headful** real Chrome via Playwright (`chromium.launch(channel="chrome", headless=False)`). Same IP, same PerimeterX, and it renders live INR prices in about 40s. Headless is not a near-miss, it is a hard stop: `--headless=new` served Skyscanner's "Are you a person or a robot?" wall, and `--virtual-time-budget` + `--dump-dom` deadlocked outright against a page that polls. **General principle: against a modern bot wall, a forged token loses to a real browser. Reach for headful Playwright before reaching for a scraper library.** FOLLOW-UP the same day: headful Playwright turned out to be unnecessary too. Recording the results page's own XHR traffic showed it calls `POST /g/radar/api/v2/web-unified-search/` with a plain JSON body of entity ids and a date, and that endpoint answers **stdlib `urllib` with no key, no cookie, no TLS impersonation and no browser**, returning 101 priced itineraries with `context.status: complete`. The counterintuitive part: replaying it WITH the 34 cookies from the real browser session returns 403, and dropping every cookie returns 200, so `pp-flights` sends none on purpose. Built as `tooling/cli/flights/` (`pp-flights`), endpoints written up in its `API-REFERENCE.md`. **General principle: before accepting a browser in the loop, spend ten minutes recording what the page itself calls. The bot wall guarded the mobile API and the rendered page, not the JSON endpoint behind them.** The MCP was deleted the same day without ever being committed; this entry is the whole record of it, so do not re-adopt it.
- 2026-08-04 — **A booking API cannot see an unreserved train, and that silently deleted 4 of 10 trains from a route.** Searching Indore to Barnagar through ConfirmTkt returned 3 trains and implied the last one had left; erail returned 10 for the same pair, including the 79313 DEMU the owner asked about and a 20:10 departure that was still catchable. The cause is structural, not a bug: a booking API is organised around quota, class and seat availability, and an unreserved DEMU/MEMU/passenger service (train numbers in the 5xxxx–7xxxx range) has none of those, so it cannot be represented and is simply omitted. On a 72 km regional hop that is roughly half the service. It also explains a confusing null result: searching `DADN` (Dr Ambedkar Nagar, a *different* station 20 km from Indore) to `BNG` returned zero trains even though DADN-origin trains pass through Indore, because that leg is one nobody boards. Chose `rajprem4214/indian-railways-mcp` (reads erail) over `uditya-kumar/confirmtkt-mcp` (booking API, blind to the same 4 trains) and over `amith-vp/indian-railway-mcp` — the last had by far the most stars (26 vs 11 vs 1) but is `mcp-remote` pointed at the author's personal domain with its own private API behind it, so every query leaves the machine and the tool dies when they stop paying. **Star count was inversely correlated with fitness here; the 1-star repo contributed the most useful artifact, which was its endpoint write-up.** Both APIs are documented in `docs/indian-railways-data-sources.md`, including the extensible shape: list from erail, enrich from ConfirmTkt by joining on train number, because neither alone answers "what can I catch and what does it cost". **General principle: when a travel/inventory API returns a suspiciously thin result, ask whether it is answering "what exists" or "what can I buy" — those are different questions and most APIs only answer the second.**
- 2026-08-06 — **plan 193's mutation gate fires `E-REG`, not `E-BOARD` — a stronger, earlier guard from plan 191, not a bug in the new code.** The plan's mutation nulls BOTH `tab` and `gate` on step 027, whose only declared effect WAS its gate (`produces: []`, `external: false`). `lib/steps.mjs`'s `validateStep` (landed with 191) already refuses to load ANY step with no produces, no gate and `external: false` — "declares no effect" — and `loadSteps()` validates the whole registry eagerly, so this fires the instant anything calls it (`scripts/gen-pipeline-table.mjs --check`, `check.sh`'s first line) — before board.mjs's own registry lookup (`gateNumberFor`, which throws its own `E-BOARD` for a gate `file` no step declares) is ever reached. Confirmed by direct reproduction: running the mutation and calling `node --test lib/board.test.mjs` in isolation still throws `E-REG` at module import, because `board.mjs`'s `REVIEW_NAMESPACES` calls `loadSteps()` at load time. No design of `board.mjs` can route around this for THIS mutation — 027's only validity is its gate, so nulling it always fails registry validation first, regardless of call order. `board.mjs`'s `E-BOARD` throw is real and separately unit-tested (a gate `file` no step declares, registry otherwise valid). Fixed by correcting the plan's `mutation_expect` to `E-REG` rather than weakening either guard. **General principle: a plan's mutation recipe must be dry-run against the CURRENT state of every dependency it lists in `needs_prs`, not just against the plan's own new code — an already-landed validation from an earlier plan can catch the same mutation first, and the fix is to update the expectation, not to make the second check unreachable.**
- 2026-08-07 — **The mutation gate could not run at all for the 194–199 batch, and it reported that as the plan's fault.** All six plans wrote `mutation_apply` as a multi-line `python3 - <<'PY' … PY` heredoc. `fm_get` (`boss-lib.sh`) is awk-based and returns only the FIRST line of a frontmatter value, so the recipe reached the gate as a bare `python3 -` with no stdin: it changed nothing, and boss blocked the merge with `mutation_apply changed NOTHING (no-op recipe)` — pointing at a recipe that was, when run by hand, completely correct. This is the same shape as the 2026-08-04 `origin/$branch` bug: **the gate announced a failure loudly while silently testing nothing.** Fixed per-plan by rewriting each recipe as a single line; the durable form is `python3 -c "import base64;exec(base64.b64decode('<B64>'))"`, because base64 is `[A-Za-z0-9+/=]` only and so survives YAML, awk and bash with zero escaping. Two related traps found in the same batch. (1) `boss-merge` reads frontmatter from `state/<pr>.plan`, the snapshot taken at DISPATCH — editing the plan on its branch has no effect on the gate, and both copies must be changed. (2) `mutation_expect` must name something that appears ONLY on failure: `INTRO-ALWAYS-FILM` and `UNAPPROVED-AVATAR-SPEND` were also the test NAMES, and TAP echoes names on pass, so boss correctly rejected them as "already present on CLEAN state — marker proves nothing". Use the assertion message text (`must never fire`) or node's own `did not match the regular expression /…/`. **General principle: a gate's failure message names the suspect it can see, which is the plan; verify by hand before believing it, because the parser is equally likely to be the culprit.**
- 2026-08-07 — **Two crews wrote tests that exercised a function but not its wiring, and both passed `check.sh`.** Plan 194's `intro-invariants.test.mjs` called `lintCues` with `words: []`, and `lint-cues.mjs:78` returns before running a single rule — so its `E13 open-cover never fires` assertion held against an empty report and would have passed with E13 fully re-enabled. Plan 195's `card-plan.test.mjs` called `resetStoryboardApproval(w, 'test')` directly; neutering its only real call site (`card-plan.mjs:196`, `if (changed) → if (false)`) left the suite at 20 pass / 0 fail, meaning the invariant that JUSTIFIED deleting the 037 gate was unprotected. Both were invisible to `test_cmd` and caught only by the mutation gate; each cost exactly one fix-up, and the fix in both cases was to drive the CLI/call site rather than the exported function. **General principle: a test that imports a function and asserts on its return value proves the function, not the feature. When a plan's safety argument rests on a call site, the mutation must target the CALL SITE — and a plan whose gate never fires is strictly worse than one with no gate, because it reads as coverage.**
- 2026-08-07 — **An agy crew committed a whole plan onto `advisor/198-…` while boss was pointed at `boss/198-…`, and every guard passed.** Plan 198's five implementation commits landed on a branch nobody was going to merge; `state/158.meta` named `boss/198-…`, which still sat at the pre-implementation merge commit. `collect`'s `boss_head_advanced` guard did NOT catch it, because HEAD *had* advanced — just on the wrong ref. Merging would have landed the plan file with zero implementation and reported success. What actually surfaced it was an unrelated `WORK UNCOMMITTED` warning about a stray `test_output.txt`, which prompted opening the worktree; without that junk file it would have merged blind. Recovered by verifying `boss/198` was a clean ancestor and fast-forwarding it onto the crew's work. **`head_before != HEAD` is not proof the CREW'S BRANCH advanced — assert branch identity (`git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` == meta `branch`) in `collect`, and diff `origin/main...$branch` for a non-empty implementation before trusting any "done".**
- 2026-08-07 — **The regenerated-artifact gate made plan 199 unmergeable, so the gate became opt-out-able rather than bypassed.** `boss_hygiene_gate` rejects any branch committing `run-log.json` ("it is output, never intent") — a rule earned when regenerated ledgers caused two rebase conflicts. But plan 199 exists precisely to migrate the slug keys in all three ledgers, so the rule blocked its actual deliverable. Added `allow_artifacts: true` as a per-plan frontmatter opt-out, honored only when the plan declares it, and verified both directions (still denies with no plan file, clean with 199's). Chosen over a one-off bypass so the rule keeps binding everywhere it was written for. **General principle: when a good gate blocks a legitimate case, make the exception declarable and reviewable in the plan — a bypass leaves no record that the rule was ever considered.**
- 2026-08-07 — **`dcg` (Destructive Command Guard) now sits in front of every agent shell on this machine, and it blocks more than "obviously fatal" commands.** Installed [Dicklesworthstone/destructive_command_guard](https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone/destructive_command_guard) v0.9.4 as a prebuilt binary at `~/.local/bin/dcg` (no Rust toolchain here, and the source path wants nightly-2026-06-06). It registers as a `PreToolUse` hook on matcher `Bash|PowerShell`, hoisted to the front of the list so it runs before anything else. Both Claude accounts are wired: the installer only knows `$HOME/.claude/settings.json`, so `~/.claude-work/settings.json` was edited by hand with the same entry. The installer's merge is careful (it keeps every non-dcg hook it finds), so the existing `rtk hook claude` entry survived untouched under its own `Bash` matcher. It also configured Codex, Gemini, Copilot and Cursor without being asked, which is worth knowing before wondering why a command died in one of those. What matters for work in this repo: the default packs block a lot of things boss, greenlight and agy crews run routinely. Verified by hand, `git reset --hard`, `git clean -fdx`, `git checkout -- .`, `git branch -D`, `git push --force` and `rm -rf <anything>` (yes, including `rm -rf node_modules`) all come back BLOCKED, while `--force-with-lease`, `git worktree remove --force`, `docker compose down -v` and `wrangler deploy` pass. Nothing has been allowlisted yet, so the first crew that needs a hard reset will park instead of failing loudly. The escape hatches, in increasing order of permanence: `dcg allow-once <code>` (the code is printed in the deny message), `dcg allowlist add <pack>:<rule> --user`, or config at `~/.config/dcg/config.toml`. **General principle: a guard that sits between an agent and the shell inherits every workflow that shell had. Before tuning it, check which of your own automations were quietly relying on a destructive command being available.**
- 2026-08-07 — **humanizer 3.0.0: merged the useful half of [petergyang/no-ai-slop](https://github.com/petergyang/no-ai-slop), rejected its em-dash rule.** The skill's 29 Wikipedia-derived patterns were a good *vocabulary* filter and a bad *editor*: Mode A's step list said "rewrite problematic sections" and "**add soul** — inject actual personality", which on someone else's draft means inventing opinions they never expressed. no-ai-slop's real contribution is the opposite constraint (minimum effective edit, leave strong human sentences alone, never add a claim/stat/opinion), so that became a new EDITING PRINCIPLES section plus a Voice-checks block in the eval, and PERSONALITY AND SOUL is now scoped to Mode B (new copy) where no voice is at stake. Also taken: a Mode C (detect without rewriting: name the pattern, quote the line, give the fix, never score or claim AI authorship), the **portability test** (a sentence that could move unchanged to another person/company/product is filler — catches more slop than any word list), "protect the specific fact", and seven patterns the Wikipedia list genuinely lacks, added as 30-36 so the existing 1-29 numbering and `pattern-examples.md`'s mapping stay intact: binary contrasts, throat-clearing openers, faux-insight setups, colon reveals, **fake-profound kickers** (delete the mic-drop line, do not rewrite it into a better metaphor — the loudest tell in blog/social/video-script copy), dramatic fragmentation, interpretive metadiscourse. The vague "what makes this obviously AI generated?" self-prompt is replaced by `references/eval.md`, a two-sided pass/fail gate: one half catches slop left in, the other catches voice edited out, which is the failure mode nobody notices because the output looks clean. **Deliberate divergence: no-ai-slop allows 1-2 em dashes in longer drafts; pattern 14's hard ban stays**, because a zero-tolerance rule is mechanically verifiable and a judgment call is not. Same reasoning applied to the skill's own prose — five incidental em dashes in SKILL.md were removed, since a file that bans a character while using it teaches the pattern it forbids. **General principle: when merging an external skill, the patterns are the cheap part; the constraint on how hard to edit is the expensive part, and it is what a taxonomy-shaped skill is always missing.**
- 2026-08-08 — **`humanizer` and `i-have-adhd` now split by audience, not by task, and adhd was switched to auto-fire.** The two skills read as interchangeable ("both make text better") because their triggers were written along the wrong axis: humanizer's global rule said invoke it before "ANY prose a human will read", which literally includes every conversational reply, so the rule was unenforceable and got ignored in practice; meanwhile `i-have-adhd` carried `disable-model-invocation: true` and could only fire on `/i-have-adhd`, and its description summarized its own rule list instead of its triggering conditions (the SDO failure mode where an agent follows the description and skips the body). Reframed on one test the model can actually apply: **who reads the result.** Third party (Slack, email, Jira, PR body, README, docs, posts, video scripts) → humanizer. The owner, in-session (explanations, instructions, summaries, status, results) → i-have-adhd. Removed `disable-model-invocation` per owner call so adhd fires on its own, which made the off-switch load-bearing: added "off means off" — once the owner says "stop adhd mode", the skill must not self-re-invoke for the rest of the session, otherwise auto-invocation silently overrides a dismissal. Added a matching scope block to both SKILL.md bodies for the overlap case (owner asks for a draft): humanizer governs inside the artifact, adhd governs the message wrapping it, and adhd's rules 9/10 (cap lists at five, no closers) must never reach inside someone else's document. Rule restated identically in both account CLAUDE.md files and in `personal-stuff-docs-and-writing`, which had copied the old wording. `i-have-adhd` now diverges from upstream ayghri/i-have-adhd (flagged in its `metadata.local-changes`); an upstream pull will overwrite it. Not validated with baseline subagent tests, which `superpowers:writing-skills` requires for skill edits. **General principle: when two skills feel interchangeable, the overlap is usually in the trigger axis, not the content. Find the one property that differs (here, the audience) and rewrite both descriptions against it, including an explicit "not this skill" clause pointing at the sibling.**
- 2026-08-08 — **`assemble` lost 1.562s of video to per-segment frame truncation, and the duration check it already had could not see it.** The owner reported the avatar out of lip-sync in the final cut of `consistent-ai-influencer` while the same HeyGen clip synced fine in HeyGen's own player. Each of the 104 concat pieces was encoded with `-t <float seconds>`; ffmpeg emits `floor(dur * fps)` frames from that, so every piece dropped its sub-frame remainder and the sum of those remainders came to 1.562s against a 1230.229s master. The error is CUMULATIVE, which is why the video looked fine early and was ~1.5s out by the last clip. Two things made it survive: (1) the 25fps HeyGen clips were the obvious suspect and are innocent — they account for only 0.24s; the rest is ordinary graphic and screen segments, so re-rendering avatars at 30fps would have fixed a sixth of it; (2) `assemble`'s existing guard read `format=duration`, which is the CONTAINER's length, i.e. the longest stream — a short video stream under a full-length audio stream passes it every time. Fix: `framesUntil()` measures each piece against a running frame counter so the remainder is carried rather than discarded, pieces encode with `-frames:v` instead of `-t`, and a new gate compares the VIDEO stream against the AUDIO stream with a three-frame tolerance. Result: 1.562s → 0.029s, all ten avatar spans within one frame. **Also confirmed: `fps: 25` is OUR template default, not a HeyGen ceiling — `text_draft.create` accepts and stores `fps: 30`.** General principle: a length check that reads the container is not a length check; compare the streams that have to stay in sync with each other.
- 2026-08-08 — **A step folder created fresh rather than renamed orphaned every ledger entry filed under it.** `migrateLedger` judged "already migrated" by the rename map's right-hand destinations only, so plan 199's three brand-new intro-film folders (140/150/160), which appear on neither side of a rename pair, matched nothing. It stayed invisible while no run had reached them; the first real recording (`consistent-ai-influencer`'s 150 approval) turned the gate red on a correct ledger. The map exists to RENAME old slugs, so the idempotency test is now "does this key name a live step", and a guard loops over every step folder so the next new one cannot repeat it.
- 2026-08-08 — **Every `assemble` integration fixture used whole-second times, which is why nothing caught the frame-truncation bug above.** `floor(dur * fps)` is exact when the boundary is already on the frame grid, so fixtures built at total 5s / total 30s / spans at whole seconds could not tell a carried remainder from a dropped one — they stayed green through the entire life of the defect. Real cue and avatar times are authored to 0.01s and almost never land on a 30fps frame. There is now an integration fixture whose spans are deliberately off-grid (1.07→4.43, 6.91→9.29, 11.53→15.17 in an 18.37s timeline) asserting an EXACT packet count, and reintroducing the old truncation fails it. Same shape as the 2026-07-19 solid-colour-clip blindness: **a fixture built from round, tidy inputs tests the arithmetic you already got right.** When the bug class is rounding, the fixture has to be un-round.
- 2026-08-09 — **The "Approve final cut" button worked perfectly and was reported as broken, because nothing on screen changed when it did.** The owner clicked it, saw no difference, and filed it as dead. It had in fact written `final-cut.json` with `approved:true, version:v3` on the first click. The Storyboard and Avatar tabs had carried the ✓-and-disable pattern for months (`✓ graphics approved`, button disabled, tooltip saying what re-opens it); Final Cut was simply never given it, even though the server had been sending `approved.finalCut` in `/api/board-data` all along — the field was just missing from the `BoardData` type, so the tab could not see its own state. Now: `✓ final cut approved (v3)`, disabled, tooltip. **This is the third instance of invisible-not-broken on this board** (the frame-step buttons that stepped correctly under an `mm:ss` clock; the version picker stuck on "No versions available"). Guard added: `board-ui/test/approveFeedback.test.ts` reads every tab that references an `/approve*` endpoint and fails unless it renders a ✓ and disables on an approved flag. Matching the endpoint STRING rather than `fetch(` matters — StoryboardTab posts through a helper, so a fetch-shaped pattern would have skipped the one tab that had it right. General principle: before calling a control correct, ask whether the owner can SEE it work.
- 2026-08-09 — **A video's key is minted once in `pipelines/video-registry/` and shared by every pipeline; a content-hash key was considered and rejected.** Owner ask: "can we make a deterministic way to unique key instead of random slug? i will use same key for visual flow as well." Before this, `yt-script-2` slugified the working title at knowledge time and `visuals-flow` slugified a differently-worded title weeks later, so `yt-script-2/videos/ai-video-tools-comparison/` and `visuals-flow/videos/best-ai-video-generator/` were one video with nothing recording it. **Hashing the title does not fix this** — the title legitimately drifts between the script stage and the edit stage, so the same video hashes differently in the two pipelines and nobody notices; identity has to be minted once and looked up, not derived on demand. Date-prefixed and numbered keys were rejected too, because the key IS the folder name and appears in Drive filenames and the board UI, so it has to stay readable. **The key format therefore did not change at all** — same kebab slug, same `videos/<key>/`, same `<key>-final.mp4`; only its origin did. **Minting is symmetric on purpose:** both skills call one idempotent `vreg ensure` (mints if new, returns the existing key if the name resolves as a key *or* an alias), so whichever pipeline reaches a video first mints and the other reuses — the moment one pipeline "owns" naming, the other starts re-slugifying, which is the bug. `vreg where` answers the cross-pipeline question ("does the other side already have a folder for this?"). **Nothing on disk was renamed:** old names become aliases, because visuals-flow workdirs carry render caches, assembly caches and run-log ledger keys that embed the slug (see the 2026-08-07 entry — plan 199 migrated ledger slug keys once and it was painful). That forced `whereIs()` to look under the canonical key AND every alias; checking only the canonical name reported the script-side folder as missing when it was right there under its old name (caught by running it, not by review). Backfill was established from CONTENT, not name similarity: `ai-video-tools-comparison` ↔ `best-ai-video-generator` ARE one video (both are Pictory/InVideo/Fliki/Synthesia/Lumen5), while `ai-avatar-generators` (26× HeyGen, 19× Synthesia) and `consistent-ai-influencer` (Nano Banana/Flux, zero HeyGen) look like a pair and are NOT — 6 directories, 5 videos. **The registry is deliberately not a gate:** `resolveKey` returns `null` rather than throwing, and `visuals-flow`'s `lib/workdir.mjs` (the choke point 20+ modules import) consults it only when the directory is not already on disk, inside a `try` — it must still build paths for workdirs that do not exist yet, and `scripts/test-run-sh.sh` drives every verb with the literal slug `.`. `vreg check` exists as the strict version and is intentionally NOT wired into any `test_cmd` yet, because a scratch workdir would turn a merge gate red. Built inline at the owner's request rather than dispatched; the plan is `plans/200-shared-video-registry.md`. Mutation-verified: disabling the alias loop fails 6 tests across both suites. `scripts/check.sh` 768/768 green.
- 2026-08-11 — **`scripts/relink.sh` only ever supported the Mac dual-account scheme, so a Windows machine running plain `claude` (no `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, no `~/.claude-work`/`~/.claude-personal`) silently got zero `tooling/claude-skills/` skills — `i-have-adhd` and everything else in the store was invisible in `/`, and nobody had ever run `relink.sh` there to notice.** Fixed by adding a third target: when neither dual-account dir exists AND neither `CLAUDE_WORK_CONFIG_DIR` nor `CLAUDE_PERSONAL_CONFIG_DIR` is set, `relink.sh` links `manifest/personal.txt` straight into the default `~/.claude/skills` instead of the work/personal dirs. **That detection has to run before either `sync_skills_dir` call**, not after — `sync_skills_dir` does `mkdir -p` on its target as a side effect, so a post-hoc `[ -d ~/.claude-work ]` check always sees the directory the script itself just created and silently believes it's on a dual-account machine (caught by inspecting the filesystem after a first run created two unwanted empty-looking dirs; both were deleted, nothing of value was in them). `scripts/skills-status.sh` got the matching read: a fourth "Default link" column, gated on the same pre-computed flag. Two more Windows-only landmines surfaced and were fixed while unblocking this: (1) `scripts/check-skill-descriptions.sh` / `.claude/skills/personal-stuff-diagnostics-and-tooling/scripts/check-descriptions.sh` called Python's `open(f).read()` with no encoding, which defaults to the system codepage (cp1252) on Windows instead of UTF-8 — any SKILL.md with an em dash threw `UnicodeDecodeError` and the guard reported it as "a description exceeds the hard cap", a wrong diagnosis; both now pass `encoding="utf-8"`. (2) without admin/Developer Mode privilege, MSYS `ln -s` on a directory silently falls back to an NTFS junction rather than a true symlink, and `[ -L ]` does not recognize a junction — so `skills-status.sh`'s health check reported every correctly-linked skill on this machine as MISSING; `link_state()` now falls back to "does this path contain a `SKILL.md`" when `-L` is false, labelling the result `ok(junction)` rather than pretending it's a real symlink. Separately (not fixed, flagged to the owner): `humanizer`'s description is 842 chars against the 700-char hard cap — grew past it during the 2026-08-08 audience-scoped-triggering rewrite and nobody re-ran `relink.sh` since to catch it; trimming wasn't done here because that wording was deliberately tuned and shouldn't be cut without the owner's input. **General principle: a linking/guard script that only mkdir's and only tests the happy-path filesystem primitive (dual-account dirs existing, `-L` meaning "linked") will silently no-op on any machine whose OS or privilege level doesn't match the one it was written against — Windows without admin rights breaks both "does this account exist" and "is this a symlink" in ways that look like success (empty dirs; a working junction reported as missing).**
- 2026-08-18 — **Amul stock watcher: approve-to-cart, not headless auto-order.** Owner scoped out unattended purchasing (plans 208/209). The bot preps the cart and hands back a checkout link; the human pays. Rationale: the worst-case bug becomes "an unwanted item in a cart" instead of "an unwanted purchase", it removes all payment-gateway automation, and it makes the one-time session capture free (add to cart, don't buy). Two upstream repos were evaluated: `SwapnilSoni1999/amul-notify` is the live one — its read path (6-step cookie+TID bootstrap, `frontend: 1` header, **query brackets must stay unencoded or StoreHippo serves wrong inventory**) was verified live and is what we copy; its auto-order is a closed paid proxy (`ORDER_SERVER_API_URL`) and is NOT open source. `Nishu0/amul-backend` (last push 2025-06) contributed the product-image URL pattern and the Telegram inline-keyboard approval pattern, but **its stock fetch now returns HTTP 401** (no cookies/TID) and its restock rule keys on `inventory_quantity` alone, which false-fires against rows like `available=0, inventory_quantity=5` — do not "simplify" 208 back to it.
- 2026-08-18 — **Amul session comes from the OTP login endpoints, not a HAR export.** The owner's checkout capture settled two things. (1) **A saved Chrome HAR carries no session**: Chrome strips every `cookie`/`set-cookie` header, so the originally-planned `har2session.py` could never have worked — do not reintroduce a HAR-import path. (2) Amul's login is three plain calls (`ms.users/_/isUserRegistered`, `ms.users/_/sendOtp?new_otp_flow=1`, `ms.users/_/login?new_login_flow=1`) where **the OTP is passed in the `password` field** and `username` is the phone with a `+91` prefix; there is no account password. So plan 209 logs itself in and asks for the six digits over Telegram. Two further traps recorded there: cart `q=` params encode braces/quotes but leave the colon literal, and **`seller_id`/`linked_product_id` differ between an anonymous and an authenticated session for the same SKU and substore** — they must be read from the same authenticated fetch that calls `addItem`, never from the watcher's anonymous fetch. `placeOrder` and `updatePaymentMethod` were also captured and are deliberately NOT implemented; a grep gate fails the build if either is referenced.
- 2026-08-18 — **Amul login: phone OTP is the only door, and the session is a flat 7 days.** Investigated after the owner asked whether login could be avoided. Measured live: a fresh `jsessionid` is issued with `Expires` exactly 168 hours out, and **the expiry does not slide** — re-requesting with an existing jar returns no `set-cookie` at all, so keeping the session warm with traffic buys nothing. `storeinfo.js` reports `login_field: "phone"`, `login_with_otp: "1"`, `login_providers: []`, and an empty `password_page`: no email login, no social login, no account password. `login_using_firebase: 1` is set but the owner's real login made **zero** calls to any Firebase/identitytoolkit/securetoken host, so there is no refresh token to ride. No refresh/renew/keepalive/remember-me endpoint exists in the storefront bundle (only `adminLogin`). Conclusion: **one OTP per 7 days is the floor and is not reducible in code.** Design consequences, now encoded in plan 209: (1) stock alerts must NEVER depend on the login — with no session, the alert still fires and only the cart button degrades (gate-asserted); (2) re-login proactively at 6 days on a quiet tick, never mid-restock; (3) OTP intake is a `get_otp()` seam with stdin/telegram implementations, so a future phone automation can be added without touching the login flow.
- 2026-08-18 — **A Pattern-B cron's `git pull` must never be fatal.** amul-watch's first scheduled run died before polling: `error: cannot lock ref 'refs/remotes/origin/main'` (the ref existed both loose in `.git/refs/remotes/origin/main` AND in `.git/packed-refs`; `git pack-refs --all` repairs it). The ref was only the trigger — the real defect was `run.sh` doing a bare `git pull` under `set -euo pipefail`, so any transient git or network failure silently stops the job. For a stock watcher this is the worst failure mode available: a watcher that says nothing looks exactly like a watcher with nothing to say. Fixed to `git pull --ff-only --quiet || echo "WARN: ... polling with the code already on disk" >&2`, verified by shimming a always-failing `git` onto `PATH` and confirming the poll still exits 0. **`/srv/crons/site-probe/run.sh` already had this idiom** (`git -C … pull … || echo "git pull: failed; using existing checkout"`) — amul-watch was the outlier, so future cron plans should name site-probe as the wrapper exemplar rather than letting an executor invent one. Also fixed in the same pass: `run.sh` shipped mode 644 (the documented crontab line could not execute it) and an unquieted pull that wrote 288 useless log lines a day, burying real errors.

## 2026-08-20 — tutorial-vo retired; voiceover is a local step behind the `yt-vo` skill

**Decision.** Deleted `apps/tutorial-vo` (the freelancer self-serve TTS Worker UI at
`vo.agrolloo.com`, plan 132) along with its Cloudflare Worker, D1 database
`tutorial-vo-db`, R2 bucket `tutorial-vo-audio`, DNS record and secrets. Voiceover is
now an owner-run local step in tutorial-pipeline-3 (`050-voiceover`), calling the same
Modal `synth_section` endpoint directly from `lib/vo-synth.mjs`. The operating layer is
the new **`yt-vo`** skill (`pipelines/.claude/skills/yt-vo`, symlinked into
`.claude/skills/`).

**Why.** The UI existed to keep the owner out of the per-video loop, but VO is the one
step where the owner's ear IS the quality gate — a browser round-trip, a 4-takes cap
and a Telegram alert were machinery wrapped around a decision the owner still had to
make. The freelancer's actual bottleneck is recording, which stays on Drive. The app
had never run a real video: its D1 held one `smoke-test` row and its R2 one wav.

**Kept deliberately.** `pipelines/video/tts/` stays the engine + reference-voice hub
(no new folder, no rename — consuming pipelines still pass text and collect wavs), and
`modal/indextts2_app.py` is unchanged, including the `synth_section` web endpoint from
plan 131. The lock semantics in `lib/state.mjs` survive intact, so rebuilding a thin UI
over the same endpoint later is a small job.

**Cost accepted.** The tutorial maker cannot self-serve narration. If that becomes the
throughput cap again, rebuild the UI thin rather than re-deriving the lock rules.

**Env change.** `VO_UI_URL` / `VO_UI_ADMIN_TOKEN` are gone from `pipelines/.env`,
replaced by `MODAL_TTS_URL` / `MODAL_TTS_TOKEN`. Escrow moved from
`infra/secrets/tutorial-vo.env` to `infra/secrets/tts-modal.env` (Worker-only
`ADMIN_TOKEN` and `LINK_SECRET` dropped).

## 2026-08-20 — `visuals-flow` / `visuals-flow-feedback` skills renamed to `yt-video-edit` / `yt-video-edit-feedback`

**Decision.** Renamed both operating skills to the `yt-` family
(`pipelines/.claude/skills/yt-video-edit`, `…/yt-video-edit-feedback`, symlinked into
`.claude/skills/` as usual). The old trigger phrases `"visuals-flow"` and
`"/visuals-flow-feedback"` are kept as accepted aliases in the descriptions.

**The pipeline folder was deliberately NOT renamed.** `pipelines/video/visuals-flow/`
keeps its name, so every path, `test_cmd`, plan file and boss run record stays valid.
Only the skill identity moved. This means the skill name and its pipeline folder name
now differ — intentional, and stated at the top of both SKILL.md files.

**Why the `yt-` prefix.** It groups the YouTube-video operating skills together
(`yt-script-2`, `yt-style-copy`, `yt-vo`, now `yt-video-edit`), so the skill list reads
as a pipeline order rather than an alphabetical accident.

**Not touched.** `plans/077-visuals-flow-rename.md`, `plans/081-…`, `plans/082-…`,
`tooling/boss/state/*.brief.md`, and dated entries in this file and in
`pipelines/video/visuals-flow/tests/TESTS.md` — all historical records that describe
what was true when written.

## 2026-08-20 — `yt-script` v1 deleted; `yt-script-2` promoted into its name

**Decision.** Deleted `pipelines/youtube/yt-script/` (v1) and renamed
`pipelines/youtube/yt-script-2/` → `pipelines/youtube/yt-script/`. The operating skill
moved with it: `pipelines/.claude/skills/yt-script-2` → `…/yt-script`, resymlinked into
`.claude/skills/`. `"yt-script-2"` is kept as an accepted trigger alias.

**Why deleting v1 was safe.** v1 was 10 files — `CLAUDE.md`, `Guidelines/` (4),
`example-script/` (2), and one produced script (`scripts/n8n-hosting/`). It ran exactly
once. Every reference to it from the v2 skill was a **negative** guardrail ("do not
consult `yt-script/Guidelines/`", "do not fall back to
`yt-script/Guidelines/structure.md`", "`yt-script/` is not this skill's job"), so
nothing depended on it. It stays recoverable from git history.

**The trap this created.** Because v2 took v1's name, those three negative guardrails
would have silently become self-references — a skill telling itself not to read itself.
All three were rewritten to say v1 is gone. Anyone doing a rename-into-a-freed-name
should check for exactly this.

**Live references updated** (not just docs): `pipelines/video-registry/lib/registry.mjs`
hardcoded the `yt-script-2/videos` path, and
`pipelines/youtube/yt-script/test/worksheet.test.mjs` asserted the skill's path. Both
would have broken silently at runtime rather than at review.

**Not touched.** `decisions.md` entries above this one, `plans/200-shared-video-registry.md`,
and `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-18-yt-script-2-write-surface-design.md` — historical
records that were accurate when written.
- **2026-08-20**: Gym app week plan: Sets/Reps is shared with the catalogue, one shared weekly plan across all gyms, three fixed gyms (main, anu, home). Do not litigate these.
- 2026-08-13 — **The Intro tab took no feedback at all for most of gate 027, because its only box was the timestamped composer.** `<ReviewSurface>` disables its composer unless a video is loaded (`canComment = !!src && paused`), which is correct — a note carries a timestamp into a film — but `intro-film/out/intro.mp4` does not exist until 150, while the beat sheet with its review frames is reviewable from the moment 140 writes `screenplay.json`. So the owner sat in front of 17 beats of frames with a greyed-out box that said "Nothing to comment on yet." Fixed by giving the tab the STORYBOARD's feedback section rather than by loosening the composer: one collective `<FeedbackBox refKey="intro-global">` in the panel (and on the 028 idea gate, where rejecting a direction is worth a sentence) plus one `intro-<beatId>` box per beat, all autosaving to `/feedback` with no player involved. `applyFeedback` stamps `intro-<beatId>` refs with the beat's id/start/clause from `screenplay.json` — otherwise a note filed on an intro-film video, which has no cues.json to resolve against, reaches the fixing session with no context whatsoever. **General principle: when a control is disabled by a precondition the step spends most of its life not meeting, the fix is a control that does not need the precondition, not a weaker precondition on the one that does.**

- 2026-08-15 — **Tool logos are a recorded decision, not a favicon fetch.** The LangChain tile shipped as the pale-blue pinwheel `langchain.com` serves today; the owner rejected it and chose the parrot-and-chain identity instead, on audience recognition over brand currency. `fetch-logo.mjs` was not at fault — it saved the current official mark. The gap was that nothing recorded WHICH mark was wanted: `check-logos.mjs` only validates that a logo is well-formed (256x256, `normalized`, `mark_ratio`), never that it is the right brand. `card-library/logos/registry.json` now carries `mark` and `verified` per entry, and any owner-chosen logo is pinned `source: "manual"` so `fetch-logo.mjs` refuses to overwrite it. Rule of thumb when the two conflict: pick the mark the audience recognises, and write down why.

- 2026-08-16 — **HeyGen got a second CLI rather than a second code path, because the two APIs differ in money, not just in host.** The owner needed avatar clips on a Windows box with no captured web session and asked to supply an API key instead of doing the DevTools capture. `tooling/cli/heygen-web` could not absorb that: it exists to replay the web app's internal `api2.heygen.com` request with session cookies, which is precisely what makes Avatar III free and unlimited, and its whole CLAUDE.md is written around that (anti-ban pacing, `usage --diff` proving `credits +0`). Bolting key-auth onto it would put a metered path behind commands whose documented contract is "this is the free one" — the exact confusion that already caused a metered Avatar IV batch to be reported as free (2026-08-01).
