Knowledge that load-bearing does not belong in a skill nobody triggers, so it became `pipelines/video/CLAUDE.md` — the folder's operate-doc, which the repo convention already says is where a folder's how-to-operate knowledge lives, and which a session working in `pipelines/video/` reads anyway. Content unchanged; only the header and the "when NOT to use this skill" section were reframed. Eight cross-references repointed across five skills (`personal-stuff-{research-methodology,video-automation-campaign,frontier,idea-to-shipped}`, `yt-vo`) plus a new root `CLAUDE.md` route. `.claude/skills/` is now 69.
- **2026-08-25 — `media-board` and all four `pinterest-*` skills archived; the Pinterest pipeline retired.** Owner's call, same audit session. Media board: "too complicated, not using it" — the localhost:4100 gallery over `~/kb-scratch/video/{tts,heygen}` is archived **with its server** (`serve.mjs` lived inside the skill folder, so archiving the skill archives the tool; six docs that told a session to "browse via the media-board skill" now point at the hub folders directly). Pinterest: owner abandoned the bet, so `pinterest-{research,make-post,board,analyze}` were archived, the `.claude/skills/pinterest-board` up-link removed, and `pipelines/pinterest/` (61 tracked files, 22 MB on disk) moved to `pipelines/archive/pinterest/`. **`apps/pinterest-landing-pages/` was deliberately NOT touched** — `keto-kitchen.agrolloo.com` and `bridebestie.com` are live Cloudflare Workers on a paid domain, and deleting the source would leave two deployed sites with no way to update them; that call belongs to the owner separately. Bet 2 in `context/bets.md` marked ABANDONED with the archive path. `.claude/skills/` 69 → 68, `pipelines/.claude/skills/` 50 → 45.

- **2026-08-25 — the Pinterest archive tripped the push gate; one pin image left the repo.** Archiving `pipelines/pinterest/` → `pipelines/archive/pinterest/` was refused by `pp-push` (`PPPUSH-SIZE`): `keto/posts/2026-06-02-7-day-keto-pathway/image.png` is 4,884,392 bytes against a 4,194,304-byte cap. The gate is right and was not raised — a generated pin for an abandoned bet is exactly the "rebuildable media must not enter a public repo" case, and the file only escaped the gate originally because it predates it. The image was copied to `~/pp-maintainer-archive/2026-08-25-pinterest-oversize/` and `git rm`'d, with `IMAGE-MOVED.md` left in its place; the other two pin PNGs are under the cap and stayed. **The lesson for any future archive-a-folder job: `git mv` of a large tree re-presents every file to the push gate, so check `git ls-files <dir>` against the cap BEFORE moving, not after the land parks.** The same land also parked once on a rebase conflict in root `CLAUDE.md`, because the workspace was claimed before the previous commit landed — claim the workspace *after* the prior land completes when both touch the same file.

- **2026-08-25 — the custody + auto-commit chain was made Windows-capable, and the interesting part is that every failure mode in it was silent.** A second operator on Windows needs `pp-work` custody, the `no-history-in-main` wall, the `commit-before-stop` nag and the post-commit lander to work; an audit of the whole chain found only four incompatibilities, but **not one of them announces itself**. (1) `shasum` is a perl script macOS ships and Git Bash usually does not; it is used at four sites (`pp-work`, `pp-land`, `pp-push`, `guard-install.sh`), and two of them derive the workspace root path from it — no hash, no workspace, no custody. Replaced with a `_sha256` wrapper that prefers `shasum` and falls back to `sha256sum`; **verified the wrapper reproduces the existing `0fbb2c25` root so no live workspace is orphaned.** (2) Both guard hooks parsed the harness payload with `python3`, which Windows generally lacks — the hooks would have exited 0 forever, i.e. the wall protecting `main` would simply not exist. They now resolve `python3 → python → py → node` (node is the reliable one: every Claude Code install carries it) and the node branch was diffed against the python branch on a payload with embedded quotes and newlines. (3) `.claude/settings.json` invoked the hooks by bare path; they are now run through an explicit `bash`, with `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` passed through `cygpath -u` where it exists so a native `C:\...` path becomes usable — the `||` branch makes it a no-op on macOS, and the exact stored command string was executed on this Mac to confirm it still blocks (exit 2) in main and permits (exit 0) in a worktree. (4) **`.gitattributes` had no `eol` rules, which is the single worst one.** Windows git defaults to `core.autocrlf=true`, so every `.sh` in the repo would check out with CRLF and die on its own shebang (`/usr/bin/env: 'bash\r': No such file or directory`) — the entire chain, disabled by a client-side default, with no repo-side evidence. LF is now pinned for all text, including the extensionless `pp-*` CLIs that git cannot infer from a suffix. **The two hooks were deliberately given opposite failure policies.** `no-history-in-main` fails CLOSED (exit 2 with an install hint) when no JSON runtime exists, because a silently-absent wall is precisely the incident it was written to prevent; `commit-before-stop` fails OPEN, because without a runtime it cannot read `stop_hook_active` either and a hook that blocks a turn it can never unblock would trap the session in a loop. The loud check for that case lives in the new **`scripts/doctor-portability.sh`** instead — one command that turns each silence into a printed FAIL (CRLF in load-bearing scripts, symlinks checked out as text files, missing hash/JSON tool, wall not blocking, lander not armed), wired non-fatally into the tail of `relink.sh` so a fresh clone reports its own state. **The 83 tracked symlinks are the one thing no repo change can fix** — they need Developer Mode plus `core.symlinks=true` on the client, so the doctor detects and explains rather than repairs. Both guard test suites still pass unchanged. **General principle: portability bugs and safety bugs are the same bug when the safety mechanism is a hook. A guard that cannot run does not fail — it disappears, and the machine it disappeared on is the one nobody is watching. Any check whose absence is invisible needs a second check whose job is to notice the absence.**
